Monthly society fee plus PetSaathi’s standard service margin from resident bookings.

Problem 3 — Resident discount can destroy margin

Correction

Use a limited, budgeted resident benefit rather than unlimited discounting.

Problem 4 — Priority matching may sound guaranteed

Correction

Problem 5 — Dedicated WhatsApp can become unlimited work

Problem 7 — No operational deliverable

A landing page and discount alone do not justify a ₹15,000 monthly fee.

Correction

Include:

SLA

Society-level reporting

Resident activation support

Simple explanation for professor

“The second package is for societies that have completed the free pilot and shown real resident demand.

The society will pay a monthly partnership fee between ₹5,000 and ₹15,000 depending on the number of homes, active pet parents, support needs and reporting complexity. Residents will still pay separately for the dog walking or pet sitting services they book.

PetSaathi will create a society-specific landing page, provide a resident benefit, maintain a local approved-access sitter roster and give booking requests priority among eligible sitters. Priority does not mean guaranteed availability because safety, experience, location and schedule must still be checked.

The society will receive a monthly report showing aggregate bookings, on-time performance, ratings and access issues. It will not receive individual pet medical details, payment data, photographs or GPS routes.

A dedicated WhatsApp support queue and named operations owner will help residents and the society resolve booking, payment, replacement and service issues. Emergency support will mean coordination with the customer and veterinary contacts, not veterinary treatment by PetSaathi.

Instead of saying ‘plus commission on bookings,’ the clearer business mod

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Package 3: Premium Pet-Care Desk 🏢🐾✨

Executive decision

Approve Package 3 with modifications.

The package is appropriate for large premium gated communities, but the phrase “dedicated society pet-care coordinator” must be defined carefully.

At ₹15,000 per month, PetSaathi can realistically provide a named shared coordinator with defined support and on-site hours. It should not promise a full-time employee exclusively stationed at one society. A genuinely full-time on-site coordinator should use custom staffing-based pricing above the standard package range.

Recommended positioning

A managed pet-care desk for premium residential communities, combining resident support, scheduled dog-walking programmes, an approved caregiver pool, partner-led pet events, safety reporting and priority emergency coordination.

Society-management platforms operating in India commonly emphasise onboarding, staff and security training, ongoing support, dashboards and a named operational contact. This supports charging a management retainer only when PetSaathi delivers real operational ownership—not merely a landing page and WhatsApp number.

1. Correct commercial structure

Society pays

Monthly Premium Pet-Care Desk retainer:

₹15,000–₹50,000

+ applicable taxes

Residents pay

Residents separately pay for:

Dog walks

Pet-sitting visits

Boarding

Grooming appointments

Training sessions

Other individually booked services

PetSaathi earns

Monthly society management fee

+

Platform contribution from completed resident bookings

+

Clearly disclosed partner-event coordination fees, where applicable

Use “service-booking revenue” or “platform service margin”, not an undefined “commission.”

The agreement and resident checkout should clearly show service descriptions, prices, cancellation terms, refund policies and grievance contacts. India’s Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules require e-commerce entities to disclose relevant transaction, payment, refund and grievance information and to process accepted refund requests.

2. Recommended package tiers

Tier A — Premium Desk Essential

Price

₹15,000–₹20,000 per month plus applicable taxes

Best for

Approximately 300–600 homes

25–50 active pet-parent households

A full-time exclusive on-site coordinator should not automatically be included at ₹50,000. It should be quoted separately based on salary, statutory employment costs, working hours, supervision, replacement coverage, travel, equipment and service scope.

3. Dedicated society pet-care coordinator

Decision: Approve with defined scope

The coordinator is the operational owner for the partnership—not a veterinarian, dog trainer, emergency responder or personal assistant for every resident.

Coordinator responsibilities

Resident support

Explain PetSaathi services

Help residents register

Assist with Pet Profiles

Explain packages and booking status

Help residents access Report Cards

Route refund and complaint requests

Society operations

Coordinate sitter security registration

Maintain the active-access roster

Review walking-slot demand

Monitor access failures

Coordinate with the facility manager

Provide aggregate programme reports

Service reliability

Monitor high-priority bookings

Review missing acknowledgements

Trigger replacement workflows

Track overdue Report Cards

Escalate active-service problems

Partner activities

Schedule grooming or vaccination camps

Verify partner readiness

Coordinate space and resident appointments

Track event incidents and feedback

Coordinator must not

Diagnose a pet

Recommend unapproved medication

Promise guaranteed veterinary availability

Override Red-risk or safety decisions

Approve refunds without finance authority

Share resident information with the committee

Assign unverified caregivers

Accept cash outside the approved payment process

Coordinator availability

Publish clear service hours:

Regular resident support:

For example, 8:00 AM–8:00 PM

Active-service urgent escalation:

According to contracted coverage

Critical emergency:

Immediate routing to the defined emergency process

“Dedicated” should mean named responsibility and priority routing, not unlimited 24/7 access.

4. Regular dog-walking slots

Decision: Approve as a core premium feature

Regular slots should create predictable supply and repeat revenue.

Example:

### Table 19

| Slot | Capacity |
| --- | --- |
| 6:30–7:00 AM | 3 individual walks |
| 7:00–7:30 AM | 3 individual walks |
| 7:30–8:00 AM | 3 individual walks |
| 6:00–6:30 PM | 3 individual walks |
| 6:30–7:00 PM | 3 individual walks |

Important safety rule

These are scheduling windows—not automatic group walks.

Dogs should not be combined merely because they use the same slot. Shared walks require separate compatibility, handling and owner-consent controls.

Recurring-slot flow

Resident selects preferred days and time

↓

Pet profile and walking controls reviewed

↓

Preferred walker checked

↓

Individual future bookings created

↓

Credits reserved

↓

Reminders scheduled

↓

Backup coverage evaluated

Slot management should include

Maximum sitter capacity

Walking duration

Handover buffer

Society-entry buffer

Sitter travel between towers

Pet size and handling controls

Same-sitter preference

Substitute approval

Public-holiday and festival rules

Customer promise

Use:

Reserved recurring time window with preferred-sitter continuity where availability permits.

Do not use:

Guaranteed same walker every day.

5. Verified walker pool

Decision: Approve

Use the term:

Society-approved-access walker pool

This represents access and platform verification—not a guarantee that risk is zero.

Walker eligibility

Each active walker should have:

Identity verification status

Relevant training

Active service permission

Society access registration

Pet-size permissions

Risk-control capabilities

Availability

Reliability status

No active safety restriction

Society/security roster

The limited roster may show:

Walker display name

PetSaathi ID

Current photograph

Approved service

Access validity

Status

PetSaathi operations contact

Do not provide the society with:

Full KYC documents

Home address

Bank details

Raw police-verification documents

Other customer bookings

Detailed performance investigations

Advertising and partnership materials must use factual and substantiated verification claims. ASCI’s code requires advertising representations to be truthful and not misleading.

6. Grooming camps

Decision: Approve as partner-led events

The Premium package may include event coordination, but the groomer remains responsible for performing the grooming service.

PetSaathi responsibilities

Screen the partner

Publish services and prices

Manage resident appointment slots

Confirm society permissions

Organise space and queue flow

Collect feedback

Route complaints

Maintain event records

Grooming-partner responsibilities

Bring trained staff and suitable equipment

Maintain hygiene

Follow handling instructions

Refuse services beyond competence

Avoid sedation

Report safety concerns

Dispose of waste appropriately

Carry any locally required permissions or insurance

Recommended initial grooming scope

Bathing

Brushing

Basic coat care

Nail trimming where appropriate

Basic hygiene services within partner competence

Do not include medical skin treatment or sedation-based grooming as ordinary camp services.

7. Vaccination camps

Decision: Approve only through registered veterinary practitioners

Vaccination is a veterinary clinical activity. PetSaathi should act as coordinator, while the registered veterinary practitioner controls assessment, consent, vaccine administration and medical records.

The Indian Veterinary Council Act regulates veterinary practice and provides for registers of veterinary practitioners. PetSaathi must verify the professional registration of the veterinary practitioner responsible for a camp.

Required controls

Registered veterinary practitioner verified

Clinic or veterinary-partner agreement

Owner consent

Pet identification

Relevant health questions

Vaccine name

Batch and expiry details

Administration date

Next-due date

Practitioner details

Veterinary certificate

Cold-chain responsibility assigned to partner

Adverse-reaction procedure

Sharps and medical-waste handling

Emergency escalation

Important messaging

Use:

Vaccination camp conducted by a registered veterinary partner.

Do not use:

PetSaathi vaccination service.

Financial boundary

The monthly package may include coordination, but vaccines, clinical examinations and certificates may be billed separately by the partner or through a clearly disclosed PetSaathi marketplace transaction.

8. Lost-pet alert system

Decision: Approve as a controlled society feature

Alert flow

Owner reports pet missing

↓

Pet identity and alert verified

↓

Society security and opted-in residents notified

↓

Sightings submitted through controlled form

↓

Owner/support contacted

↓

Alert closed when pet is found

Alert information

May include:

Pet name

Current photograph

Species and description

Last-seen time

Last-seen society area

Safe approach guidance

Masked contact or PetSaathi support link

Do not publish

Exact home number

Owner’s direct phone by default

Medical records

Full tracking history

Permanent public link

Family emergency contacts

Alert states

DRAFT

ACTIVE

SIGHTING_REPORTED

OWNER_CONTACTED

FOUND

EXPIRED

CANCELLED

FALSE_REPORT_REVIEW

The society pet directory and lost-pet alerts must remain opt-in and purpose-limited. India’s final DPDP Rules were published on November 14, 2025 with staged commencement provisions, so PetSaathi should implement clear notices, access restrictions, security and retention controls according to the provisions applicable at launch.

Decision: Approve with event limits

The package should define exactly how many events are included.

The monthly report should use role-based access and data minimisation. The society needs programme-level operational information, not full access to PetSaathi’s resident database.

Response time

12. Pricing viability

Why ₹15,000 is not enough for full-time exclusivity

The monthly retainer must cover some combination of:

Coordinator time

Operations supervision

WhatsApp/support workload

Society setup

Security training

Monthly reporting

Events

Technology

Replacement management

Incident readiness

Travel

Administrative overhead

Therefore:

₹15,000–₹20,000: shared coordinator with limited on-site availability

₹25,000–₹35,000: named managed coordinator with regular on-site hours

₹40,000–₹50,000: enhanced concierge programme, but still not necessarily a full-time exclusive employee

Full-time exclusive desk: custom commercial quotation

This pricing distinction is a business-model recommendation based on delivery scope. Society-management providers’ own descriptions show that onboarding, staff/security training, resident support and direct operational ownership are meaningful service components that need capacity and staffing.

13. Illustrative unit economics

The following model is hypothetical and should be replaced with actual Phase 7 data.

Managed package example

Society retainer: ₹30,000

50 monthly dog walks × ₹49 margin: ₹2,450

20 package walks × ₹40 margin: ₹800

10 pet-sitting services × ₹120: ₹1,200

Partner coordination contribution: ₹1,500

Total gross contribution: ₹35,950

Estimated direct monthly programme costs:

Coordinator allocation: ₹12,000

Support allocation: ₹4,000

On-site travel: ₹2,000

Reporting/admin: ₹2,000

Event allocation: ₹3,000

Technology/payment overhead: ₹2,000

Estimated direct cost: ₹25,000

Estimated contribution: ₹10,950

Important conclusion

The retainer should fund baseline management. Booking volume should improve profitability—not subsidise a heavily underpriced “dedicated” desk.

14. Contract and billing rules

Recommended initial term

Three-month minimum following a successful pilot

For the highest tier, a six-month programme may be appropriate after the first review.

Contract should state

Package tier

Coordinator availability

On-site hours

Included events

Support hours

Active services

Resident pricing model

Emergency-coordination limits

Data-sharing rules

Branding permission

Reporting schedule

Termination

Payment due date

Additional charges

Event cancellation

Partner responsibilities

Invoicing

The society partnership fee and resident booking charges should be invoiced separately.

CBIC rules require tax invoices for taxable service supplies to contain prescribed information and generally require the invoice for services to be issued within 30 days of supply. The exact GST classification and tax treatment should be confirmed with a chartered accountant.

Correction

Define quarterly or monthly allowances by tier.

Correction

State:

Residents pay for booked services; PetSaathi retains its disclosed platform service contribution.

Problem 8 — No capacity limit

Correction

Each package should define:

Maximum active pet households

Number of gates/towers

18. Renewal and downgrade rules

Renew Premium Desk when

25+ active paying households

Strong recurring-plan demand

Positive programme contribution

Society values the coordinator

Events generate useful engagement

Complaints are manageable

Security-entry process works

Committee wants continuation

Downgrade to Basic Partner when

Booking activity is moderate

On-site coordinator demand is low

Events have weak participation

Society uses mainly the portal and WhatsApp support

Pause or terminate when

Society repeatedly blocks approved sitters

Resident demand is absent

Payment remains overdue

Society requests unlawful or discriminatory pet restrictions

Sensitive data is misused

Safety procedures are obstructed

The programme remains financially unsustainable

AWBI publishes guidance for pet owners, caregivers, RWAs and apartment associations. PetSaathi’s partnership should support responsible coexistence and should not be used to implement blanket pet bans or unlawful animal-control policies.

Final approved package

PetSaathi Premium Pet-Care Desk

Best for

Large, premium gated communities with demonstrated pet-parent demand, recurring booking density and complex society-access requirements.

| Premium Desk Managed | ₹25,000–₹35,000 + taxes |
| Premium Resident Concierge | ₹40,000–₹50,000 + taxes |
| Full-time exclusive desk | Custom quote |

Final operating principle

The Premium Pet-Care Desk should be priced as a managed local operation—not as a collection of promotional features. The monthly retainer pays for coordination, access, support, reporting and service reliability; residents continue to pay for the care services they use.

Simple explanation for professor

“The Premium Pet-Care Desk is designed for large gated societies with many pet-owning families.

The society will pay between ₹15,000 and ₹50,000 per month depending on its size, number of pet parents, support hours, events and operational complexity. At the lower price, the coordinator will be shared and available for defined hours. A full-time coordinator working only for one society will require separate custom pricing.

The coordinator will help residents register, manage regular walking schedules, coordinate sitter access, monitor service problems and prepare the monthly society report.

Residents can book regular dog-walking slots from a verified local walker pool. These slots will improve repeat bookings, but the system will still check pet behaviour, sitter availability and safety before confirmation.

Grooming camps will be conducted through verified grooming partners. Vaccination camps will be conducted only by registered veterinary practitioners. PetSaathi will organise the camp but will not perform veterinary treatment.

The lost-pet alert will notify society security and opted-in residents while protecting the owner’s private information. Society events may include pet-care awareness, training workshops, grooming camps or veterinary-partner sessions.

The monthly safety report will show aggregate information such as completed services, punctuality, entry issues and incidents. It will not expose individual pet medical records, GPS routes or resident complaints.

Priority emergency support means that PetSaathi will quickly create an incident record, contact the owner and coordinate with veterinary or transport partners. It does not mean that PetSaathi provides veterinary diagnosis or guarantees an ambulance.”

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Package 4: Society Event & Camp Model 🎪🐾

Executive decision

Approve this package with important modifications.

The Event/Camp Model can generate many qualified pet-parent leads quickly, but PetSaathi should operate primarily as the:

Event organiser

+ booking platform

+ resident communication layer

+ partner-verification layer

+ payment and support coordinator

Specialist providers remain responsible for delivering specialist services:

A registered veterinary practitioner controls health examinations, clinical advice and vaccination.

A verified groomer performs grooming.

A screened trainer conducts the training workshop.

The sponsor funds or supports the event under a clearly disclosed commercial arrangement.

The recommended public name is:

PetSaathi Society Pet-Care Event & Camp Programme

This package may be sold independently or included as an add-on to the Basic Society Partner and Premium Pet-Care Desk packages.

1. Corrected event revenue model

### Table 25

| Event | Recommended commercial model |
| --- | --- |
| Pet health-check camp | Fixed coordination fee or fee per completed appointment |
| Grooming camp | Fixed partner fee or percentage of completed net service value |
| Vaccination camp | Fixed event-management fee; avoid incentives based on vaccine quantity |
| Pet-parent meet-up | Transparent sponsorship plus optional stall fees |
| Dog-training workshop | Ticket revenue split with trainer |
| Follow-up bookings | Normal PetSaathi service contribution |

Important correction

Avoid using only the word commission.

Every agreement should specify:

Who charges the resident

Who issues the invoice

Who delivers the service

What PetSaathi earns

What the partner earns

Who handles refunds

Who handles complaints

Who carries clinical or professional responsibility

Without this clarity, an event dispute can become a payment, consumer-support and liability problem.

2. Package purpose

This package has four business goals:

Acquire opted-in pet-parent leads.

Create trust through physical society presence.

Generate direct event revenue.

Convert attendees into recurring PetSaathi customers.

The real funnel is:

Society residents reached

↓

Event registrations

↓

Residents who attend

↓

Pet Profiles created

↓

Trial service booked

↓

Paid service completed

↓

Repeat walking or sitting plan

Event attendance alone is not business success.

3. Pet health-check camp

Decision: Approve only through a registered veterinary partner

A pet health-check camp may involve:

Basic physical examination

Weight and body-condition observation

Dental or skin observations

Vaccination-record review

General preventive-care advice

Referral for further examination

PetSaathi should not perform or market these as its own clinical services.

The Indian Veterinary Council Act regulates veterinary practice and gives registered veterinary practitioners the right to practise veterinary medicine. It also restricts veterinary practice by unregistered persons, subject to limited state-authorised minor services under registered-veterinarian supervision.

Recommended operating model

Resident registers pet

↓

Veterinary consent form completed

↓

Pet identity confirmed

↓

Registered veterinarian performs examination

↓

Veterinarian records findings

↓

Resident receives clinical summary or referral

↓

PetSaathi receives only permitted operational data

PetSaathi responsibilities

Verify the veterinarian’s registration.

Obtain society permission.

Manage appointment slots.

Provide resident notices.

Control queues and waiting areas.

Process bookings where contractually agreed.

Route complaints to the correct provider.

Maintain event and payment records.

Offer follow-up PetSaathi services separately.

Veterinary partner responsibilities

Clinical examination

Clinical advice

Medical records

Prescription or treatment decisions

Professional consent

Clinical waste handling

Emergency response relating to the examination

Referral to a clinic where necessary

Recommended revenue model

Use one of these:

Fixed event coordination

Veterinary partner pays PetSaathi:

₹5,000–₹15,000 proposed coordination fee

Completed appointment fee

₹100–₹300 proposed fee

per completed, verified appointment

Society-sponsored camp

Society or sponsor pays event cost

Residents receive free or subsidised checks

Veterinarian remains clinically responsible

A fixed coordination fee is generally cleaner than taking a percentage of medical fees because it reduces the appearance of incentives to recommend unnecessary clinical activity.

4. Grooming camp

Decision: Approve as a partner-led commercial event

The grooming partner should provide:

Trained groomers

Equipment

Cleaning materials

Safe restraint practices

Hygiene and disinfection

Staff supervision

Service-specific consent

Incident reporting

Recommended launch services

Bathing

Brushing

Basic coat care

Nail trimming where appropriate

Basic hygiene services

Limited ear cleaning within partner competence

Do not include as ordinary camp services:

Sedation

Medical skin treatment

Wound treatment

Veterinary dental procedures

Complex services outside the partner’s competence

Recommended revenue model

Percentage model

PetSaathi:

15–25% proposed share

Grooming partner:

75–85% proposed share

Calculate the split on a clearly defined amount:

Net completed service value

after approved discounts and refunds

before or after tax as specified in the agreement

Fixed appointment model

PetSaathi receives:

₹100–₹300 proposed fee

per completed grooming appointment

Fixed event-management model

Partner or society pays:

₹7,500–₹20,000 proposed event fee

The fixed-fee model is often easier when grooming prices vary significantly by pet size, coat and service.

5. Vaccination camp

Decision: Approve only through a registered veterinary practitioner

Vaccination should be clinically controlled by the veterinary partner.

The Indian Veterinary Council Act places veterinary practice with registered veterinary practitioners, while limited vaccination services by specified assistants depend on state authorisation and registered-veterinarian supervision. PetSaathi’s safest national policy is therefore to require a registered veterinary practitioner to control every vaccination camp.

Required controls

Veterinary registration verified

Owner consent

Pet identity

Relevant health questions

Vaccine name

Batch number

Expiry date

Administration date

Next due date

Veterinary clinic/practitioner

Certificate or record

Adverse-reaction workflow

The veterinary partner should remain responsible for:

Vaccine sourcing

Storage

Temperature control

Administration

Medical suitability

Sharps and biomedical waste

Adverse-event response

Government animal-health programmes explicitly treat cold-chain infrastructure and vaccine logistics as required components of vaccination delivery, reinforcing the need to assign these responsibilities to the veterinary partner.

Recommended revenue model

Avoid:

PetSaathi receives commission

for every vaccine dose sold

That can create the wrong clinical incentive.

Prefer:

Fixed event-management fee

₹7,500–₹25,000 proposed

depending on size and operational scope

Fixed completed-registration fee

₹100–₹250 proposed

per completed resident appointment

Sponsor-funded camp

A pet-health brand or society may subsidise the event, but the sponsor must not control the veterinarian’s clinical decisions.

Public wording

Use:

Vaccination camp conducted by a registered veterinary partner and coordinated by PetSaathi.

Do not use:

PetSaathi vaccinates your pet.

6. Pet-parent meet-up

Decision: Approve as a community and sponsorship event

This event can include:

Pet-parent orientation

Sitter introductions

Responsible pet-care awareness

PetSaathi service demonstration

Lost-pet preparedness

Society access rules

Veterinary-partner Q&A

Sponsor booths

Resident networking

Sponsorship revenue

Proposed sponsorship levels:

### Table 26

| Sponsor level | Proposed price |
| --- | --- |
| Supporting sponsor | ₹10,000–₹20,000 |
| Main sponsor | ₹25,000–₹50,000 |
| Presenting sponsor | ₹50,000+ for a major event |

These are proposed commercial bands, not statutory or universal market prices.

Sponsorship controls

A sponsor may receive:

Approved logo placement

Event booth

Short educational presentation

Product samples with resident consent

Aggregate attendance information

Opt-in lead referrals where clearly disclosed

A sponsor must not automatically receive:

Resident phone numbers

Pet medical details

Booking histories

Exact apartment details

Emergency contacts

Non-consenting attendee information

Sponsored digital posts and promotional material should clearly identify the commercial relationship. ASCI permits labels such as “Sponsored,” “Partnership,” “Ad” and “Collaboration” and requires promotional content to be identifiable as advertising.

Recommended disclosure

This event is supported by [Sponsor]. PetSaathi does not share resident contact information with sponsors unless the resident separately opts in.

7. Dog-training workshop

Decision: Approve as a humane, trainer-led educational workshop

Recommended topics:

Leash handling

Recall foundations

Puppy socialisation

Understanding body language

Enrichment

Separation preparation

Safe introductions

Common-area behaviour

Reward-based training foundations

Trainer verification

Record:

Identity

Training background

Experience

References

Methods used

Service scope

Incident history

Insurance, where available

Prohibited techniques

Prohibited event practices

Physical punishment

Intimidation-based demonstrations

Forced interactions

Uncontrolled off-leash groups

Guaranteed behavioural “cures”

Breed-based conclusions

Medical diagnosis

Humiliating or entertainment-focused animal tricks

The Prevention of Cruelty to Animals Act is intended to prevent unnecessary pain or suffering. Its performing-animal chapter also regulates ticketed exhibitions and training for such exhibitions. An ordinary educational workshop is not necessarily the same as an animal performance, but a ticketed show built around animals performing for entertainment should receive specific legal review.

The agreement should specify whether payment-provider fees, taxes, refunds, venue cost and marketing cost are deducted before the split.

9. End-to-end event workflow

Stage 1 — Event proposal

Define:

Stage 4 — Resident registration

Collect only necessary information:

Resident name

Contact

Society/unit verification

Pet name and species

Selected slot

Relevant service information

Consent

Payment

Medical information should be collected only when necessary for the veterinary service and accessed only by authorised personnel.

The DPDP Rules, 2025 were published in November 2025 with staged commencement provisions. Event registration should therefore include a clear notice describing what information is collected, why it is needed, who receives it and how long it will be retained.

Stage 5 — Event-day check-in

QR or booking verification

↓

↓

↓

↓

↓

Stage 6 — Payment and invoicing

The system should distinguish:

Society event fee

Resident service fee

Partner professional fee

Sponsor payment

Refund

PetSaathi coordination fee

Registered service suppliers must issue invoices containing required service, value and tax information; CBIC’s invoice rules also specify timelines and invoice particulars for taxable services.

The final GST classification and tax treatment should be confirmed with a chartered accountant.

Stage 7 — Post-event conversion

Within 24–48 hours:

Thank attendee

↓

Send service summary or partner record

↓

Request feedback

↓

Offer PetSaathi trial

↓

Offer pet-profile completion

↓

Offer walking/sitting plan

Animal interaction rules

Use scheduled slots.

Avoid overcrowding.

Do not judge the event only by same-day ticket revenue. A high-quality event can create more value from subsequent dog-walking plans.

14. What is wrong in the original proposal?

Problem 1 — “Vet partner commission” is too vague

Correction

Use a fixed event fee or completed-appointment fee and keep clinical responsibility with the registered veterinarian.

Problem 2 — Vaccination commission could create a clinical incentive

Correction

Do not link PetSaathi income directly to the number or type of vaccines recommended. Use fixed coordination pricing.

Problem 3 — Grooming revenue share lacks a calculation base

Correction

Define whether the share applies to gross payment, net completed service value, or amount after refunds and taxes.

Problem 4 — Sponsorship can become hidden advertising

Correction

Disclose sponsors clearly and require separate resident consent before sponsor lead sharing.

Problem 5 — Ticketed training can become an animal show

Correction

Keep it educational and humane. Obtain legal review where the format involves ticketed animal performances.

Problem 6 — No medical, privacy or incident controls

Correction

Every event requires professional responsibility, resident notice, consent, access restrictions and incident handling.

Problem 7 — Leads are counted without consent or conversion

Correction

Track opt-in leads through paid and repeat bookings.

Final approved package

PetSaathi Society Event & Camp Programme

Best for

New-society acquisition

Resident lead generation

Partnership activation

Pet-parent trust building

Partner-service testing

Converting residents into recurring care customers

Approved services

Veterinary-partner health-check camps

Grooming-partner camps

Registered-veterinarian vaccination camps

Sponsored pet-parent meet-ups

Humane trainer-led workshops

Revenue

Society coordination fee

+

Sponsor revenue

+

Event ticket revenue

+

Transparent partner fee/revenue share

+

Post-event PetSaathi booking contribution

Not permitted

PetSaathi staff practising veterinary medicine

Hidden sponsor promotion

Uncontrolled animal gatherings

Sharing resident lists without opt-in

Vaccination incentives based on clinical recommendations

Cruel or entertainment-based training

Unclear partner responsibility

Serious incidents stored only in WhatsApp

Final operating principle

The event should generate trust and qualified demand, but professional partners must remain responsible for the specialist services they deliver. PetSaathi owns coordination, booking, transparency, support and follow-up—not veterinary or specialist decisions.

Simple explanation for professor

“Package 4 is an event and camp model used to acquire many pet-parent leads inside apartment societies.

PetSaathi can organise health-check camps, grooming camps, vaccination camps, pet-parent meetings and dog-training workshops. However, PetSaathi will act mainly as the organiser and booking platform.

Health checks and vaccination will be conducted only through registered veterinary practitioners. Grooming will be performed by verified grooming partners, and training workshops will be conducted by screened trainers using humane methods.

PetSaathi can earn through event-coordination fees, sponsor payments, training tickets, grooming revenue share and future dog-walking or pet-sitting bookings. Medical-event income should normally use fixed coordination fees rather than commission based on the number of vaccines or treatments.

Residents must voluntarily register and receive a clear privacy notice. Sponsors and societies will not automatically receive resident phone numbers or pet medical data.

After the event, PetSaathi will invite attendees to complete their Pet Profiles, book a trial service and purchase repeat walking or sitting plans. Therefore, event success will be measured not only by attendance, but also by paid bookings and repeat customers.”

A monthly compliance watch for veterinary, privacy, advertising and municipal event rules would help before expanding camps into additional cities.

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Society-Specific Landing Pages 🏢🐾

Executive decision

Approve the society landing-page feature, but each page must be a real operational page—not a template where only the society name changes.

Recommended route structure:

/pet-care/societies/[city]/[society-slug]

Examples:

/pet-care/societies/ahmedabad/green-acres-bopal

/pet-care/societies/bengaluru/prestige-lakeside-whitefield

/pet-care/societies/gurugram/dlf-phase-5

This structure is clearer than placing every society directly below /society/, because it provides a logical hierarchy:

Pet Care

↓

City

↓

Society

Each page should answer four questions immediately:

Is PetSaathi active in this society?

Which services are currently available?

Which trust and access controls are in place?

How can a resident book safely?

1. Critical SEO warning

Do not automatically publish hundreds of near-identical society pages.

Google defines doorway abuse as creating many substantially similar pages targeting specific queries while providing little unique value. Google also warns that generating large numbers of pages without meaningful original value may violate its scaled-content policies.

Wrong approach

Green Acres pet-care page

Prestige Lakeside pet-care page

DLF Phase 5 pet-care page

with exactly the same text except for:

Society name

City name

Area name

Correct approach

Index a society page only when it contains genuine society-specific information, such as:

Active services

Confirmed access process

Actual service timings

Available local sitter pool

Society-specific resident offer

Local veterinary partners

Real operating contact

Society-specific FAQ

Actual booking capacity

Genuine programme metrics

Google’s search systems are designed to prioritise useful, reliable, people-first pages rather than content produced primarily to manipulate rankings.

2. Society-page publishing states

Every society should have a publishing state.

DRAFT

IN_REVIEW

PILOT

ACTIVE

PAUSED

ENDED

ARCHIVED

Recommended indexing rules

### Table 30

| Society status | Public page | Search indexing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Draft | No | No |
| In review | No | No |
| Pilot with limited information | Optional | Usually noindex |
| Active with complete content | Yes | Index |
| Temporarily paused | Yes, with notice | Case-specific |
| Partnership ended | Redirect or archived notice | Usually remove/index carefully |
| Duplicate/renamed society | Redirect | Canonical destination |

Only active and sufficiently complete pages should appear in the sitemap.

Google recommends using canonical URLs to identify the preferred version when duplicate or highly similar URLs exist.

3. Recommended page structure

Section 1 — Hero and society identity

Purpose

Create immediate local trust and confirm that the resident has reached the correct society page.

Recommended content

PetSaathi for Green Acres, Bopal

Verified pet-care support for Green Acres residents.

Book approved-access dog walkers and pet sitters with

service updates, Report Cards and responsive support.

Trust indicators

Display factual indicators such as:

Society partnership: Active

Society access process: Configured

Local sitter pool: 4 active caregivers

Services: Dog walking and pet sitting

Support: Available during published hours

Avoid

Guaranteed sitter

Risk-free services

Society-certified perfect walkers

Verification claims should describe exactly what was checked.

Do not show services that are unavailable in that society.

Use:

rather than implying universal availability.

8. Section 6 — How society booking works

A small visual timeline improves clarity.

1. Create Pet Profile

2. Choose service and schedule

3. PetSaathi reviews care requirements

4. Eligible local sitter is assigned

5. Payment is verified

6. Society access is prepared

7. Service is completed

8. Report Card is delivered

Important statement

A booking is confirmed only after sitter assignment, payment verification and required safety checks.

This prevents customers from assuming that submitting the form guarantees service.

Show the actual operating controls configured for that society.

Example

Do not publish security phone numbers, visitor codes or entry credentials publicly.

10. Section 8 — Emergency support

Correct terminology

Use:

Do not use:

unless PetSaathi directly operates a properly qualified veterinary service.

[Call Support]

be guaranteed.

A PetSaathi support number

Personal coordinator numbers

11. Section 9 — Society-specific FAQ

FAQs should address real questions from that society.

Recommended questions

Is PetSaathi officially active in Green Acres?

Explain the current partnership or pilot status.

Which services are available?

Show active services and limits.

How does a sitter enter the society?

Explain the configured access process.

Is the same sitter guaranteed?

Explain same-sitter preference versus availability.

Is payment required before confirmation?

Explain prepaid confirmation.

Can I book for more than one pet?

Explain multi-pet profiles and charges.

What happens when the sitter cancels?

Explain the replacement and refund process.

Who can see my address and Pet Profile?

Explain role-based access.

Is GPS available for every service?

Explain that basic tracking applies mainly to dog walks.

What happens during a pet emergency?

Explain escalation and veterinary coordination.

Important SEO rule

Do not copy the same FAQ word for word across every society page.

Include genuine questions such as:

Which gate should walkers use?

Are morning slots available?

Is boarding currently active?

How are replacement sitters approved?

Are multiple towers supported?

15. Next.js implementation

Route structure

app/

└── pet-care/

└── societies/

└── [city]/

└── [societySlug]/

├── page.tsx

├── loading.tsx

├── not-found.tsx

└── opengraph-image.tsx

Next.js dynamic route segments are designed for routes whose values come from data, such as city and society slugs.

Recommended server-side page flow

Read city and society slug

↓

Load society record

↓

Confirm public status

↓

Load active services

↓

Load public sitter summaries

↓

Load resident benefit

↓

Load partner summaries

↓

Load society FAQs

↓

Render page

Static generation

Use generateStaticParams() for active society pages when the set is known and changes moderately. Next.js supports combining dynamic routes with generateStaticParams to generate specified routes at build time.

Dynamic metadata

Use generateMetadata() to create page-specific:

Title

Description

Canonical URL

Open Graph title

Open Graph image

Robots rules

Next.js supports route-dependent metadata through generateMetadata, including dynamic values based on route parameters.

16. Recommended metadata

Title

Pet Care in Green Acres Bopal | Dog Walking & Pet Sitting | PetSaathi

Description

PetSaathi provides verified dog walking and in-home pet sitting

for Green Acres Bopal residents, with service updates, Report Cards

and society-access caregivers.

Canonical

https://petsaathi.in/pet-care/societies/ahmedabad/green-acres-bopal

Open Graph

PetSaathi × Green Acres Bopal

Trusted pet care for your society

Google recommends descriptive, relevant page titles and may use several page signals when generating title links in search results.

17. Structured data

Recommended

BreadcrumbList

Use on each society page.

Organization

Use primarily on the PetSaathi homepage to identify PetSaathi’s organisation details and logo. Google specifically recommends organisation markup on the home page for organisational identification.

Generic WebPage and Service

These may help describe the page semantically, but not every schema.org type produces a Google rich result. Google maintains a defined gallery of structured-data features it supports for rich search appearances.

Do not misuse LocalBusiness

A residential society page is not automatically a PetSaathi business branch.

Use LocalBusiness only where PetSaathi has a genuine local business presence with accurate:

Address

Opening hours

Contact

Operational identity

Google’s LocalBusiness markup is intended to describe actual business details such as business location and operating hours.

Do not mark the society’s address as a PetSaathi office merely for local SEO.

Validation

Validate structured data with:

Google Rich Results Test

Schema.org validator

Search Console URL Inspection

Google recommends testing structured data and fixing critical validation errors before broader deployment.

18. Sitemap and indexing

Generate the sitemap only from active, indexable society pages.

ACTIVE + content_complete + public = sitemap inclusion

Do not add:

Draft pages

Empty society templates

Ended partnerships

Pages with no active service

Internal preview URLs

Google uses sitemaps to discover important pages and understand information such as update dates. Next.js supports generating dynamic sitemap files through its metadata file conventions.

19. Mobile-first design

Most society users will likely arrive from:

WhatsApp

QR posters

Mobile search

Society communication apps

The page should therefore be designed mobile-first.

Mobile layout order

Society identity

Services

Resident benefit

Book CTA

Trust controls

Available sitter summaries

How it works

Report Card preview

Emergency support

FAQs

Partners

Sticky action bar

[Book Now] [WhatsApp Support]

Avoid blocking the page immediately with a full-screen sign-up popup.

Google recommends responsive design and warns that intrusive interstitials can harm usability and make content harder for users and search engines to access.

20. Performance requirements

Avoid loading:

Full Google Maps SDK above the fold

Original sitter photographs

Autoplay videos

Every partner image immediately

Large tracking demonstrations

Multiple analytics systems before content

Recommended:

Optimised responsive images

Lazy-loaded maps

Server-rendered primary content

Small sitter-card images

Deferred report preview media

Cached public society data

Google recommends good Core Web Vitals for loading, interactivity and visual stability as part of a strong user experience.

21. Privacy and data protection

The public page must not expose private resident or booking data.

Public information

Society name

Area

Active services

General service windows

Public sitter summaries

Resident benefit

Aggregate metrics

Authenticated resident information

Pet Profile

Private Report Card

Photos/videos

GPS route

Refund status

Complaints/incidents

Society-only aggregate information

Completed service counts

On-time rate

Access issues

Aggregate complaints

Programme status

The DPDP Act and final DPDP Rules establish the current Indian digital-personal-data framework, including published rules and enforcement timelines. Society onboarding and landing-page forms should therefore use clear notices, limited data collection, controlled access and defined retention.

25. What is wrong in many society landing pages?

Mistake 1 — Only the society name changes

Correct

Every indexed page must contain actual local operating information.

Mistake 2 — Fake local supply

Wrong

“20 sitters near your society”

when none are currently eligible.

Correct

Display only active and verified local capacity.

Mistake 3 — Showing unavailable services

Correct

Use explicit service statuses and waiting lists.

Mistake 4 — Emergency support is exaggerated

Correct

Describe veterinary coordination and actual support hours.

Mistake 5 — Society address is used as a fake PetSaathi branch

Correct

Do not misuse LocalBusiness structured data.

Mistake 6 — Resident discount has hidden conditions

Correct

Display eligibility and final price clearly.

Mistake 7 — The page exposes sensitive access information

Correct

Keep gate codes, unit numbers and personal contacts behind authentication.

Mistake 8 — Hundreds of thin pages are indexed

Correct

Publish only complete, active, unique pages and apply noindex to incomplete pilot pages.

Final approved page blueprint

1. Breadcrumb

2. Society hero

3. Partnership and availability status

4. Services available

5. Resident benefit

6. Booking CTA

7. How booking works

8. Trust and safety controls

9. Local sitter summaries

10. Society access information

11. Report Card demonstration

12. Programme results

13. Emergency coordination

14. Society-specific FAQs

15. Local vet/groomer partners

16. Final booking CTA

17. Privacy, cancellation and support links

Definition of done

A society landing page is ready only when:

Operational accuracy

Society partnership status is correct.

Services shown are genuinely active.

Sitter summaries reflect real capacity.

Resident benefit is valid.

Support contact is working.

Access procedure is verified.

Conversion

Booking CTA is visible immediately.

Society is preselected.

Pricing is clear.

Mobile booking works.

WhatsApp is available as support, not the only workflow.

SEO

Page has substantial unique content.

Title and description are society-specific.

Canonical URL is correct.

Active page is included in sitemap.

Incomplete pages are not indexed.

Breadcrumb structured data validates.

Privacy

No resident information is public.

No private sitter document is exposed.

No exact GPS route is public.

Society receives only aggregate programme data.

Forms include appropriate data-use notices.

Performance

Hero content loads quickly.

Images are optimised.

Maps load only when required.

Mobile layout is stable.

Primary content is server-rendered and crawlable.

Final operating principle

A society landing page should be a live service interface for residents—not a mass-produced SEO page. It must accurately represent local supply, access, pricing, safety controls and booking availability.

Simple explanation for professor

“The society landing page will be a personalised web page for each active apartment society.

For example, Green Acres Bopal will have its own page showing the pet-care services available inside that society, the local sitter pool, the resident offer, booking process, support contact and society-specific rules.

The page will not simply replace one society name with another. Every page must contain real local details such as service timings, access rules, available sitters, partner veterinarians and frequently asked questions.

Residents can choose dog walking or pet sitting, create their Pet Profile and submit a booking directly from the page. The society will already be selected, so residents do not need to enter the complete location again.

Only public sitter information will appear on the page. Personal documents, addresses, phone numbers and disciplinary information will remain private.

The page will include emergency veterinary coordination, but PetSaathi will not claim to provide veterinary treatment. It will also show only active services and genuine availability.

For search visibility, only complete and active society pages will be indexed. Empty or copied pages will not be published because large numbers of similar pages can be treated as low-value doorway content.

The final goal is to make every society page a trusted local booking portal that improves resident conversion and reduces operational confusion.”

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Society Resident Verification, Sitter Pool and Pet-Care Calendar 🏢🐾

Executive decision

Approve all three features, with these corrections:

### Table 32

| Feature | Decision | Important correction |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Society resident verification | Approve | Use layered verification; a flat number or WhatsApp number alone is insufficient |
| Society sitter pool | Approve | Use capacity rules and service permissions, not fixed headcounts only |
| Society pet-care calendar | Approve | Separate private service slots from public society events |

The final operating structure should be:

Resident verifies society membership

↓

Resident creates Pet Profile

↓

System shows society-supported services and calendar

↓

Booking is matched to the approved local sitter pool

↓

Gate access, payment and safety checks complete

↓

Service proceeds

2. Society resident verification

Goal

Confirm that a user is genuinely connected to a society before providing:

Society-specific resident discounts

Private society booking slots

Internal pet-parent community access

Society event registration

Lost-pet alerts

Society-specific support

Access to resident-only sitter information

Verification should not be used to block a resident from ordinary public PetSaathi services. It only controls access to society-specific benefits and private community features.

Recommended verification principle

Do not rely on one field.

Weak verification

Flat number entered

↓

User immediately marked verified

Anyone who knows a tower and flat number could misuse this flow.

Correct verification

User selects “I live in this society”

↓

Phone number verified

↓

Tower/block and flat entered

↓

Society-specific proof checked

↓

Verification status assigned

A WhatsApp OTP confirms control of a phone number; it does not independently prove that the person lives in the society.

Verification methods

Method A — Society invite link

Best for controlled resident onboarding.

Society sends approved invitation

↓

Resident opens signed link

↓

Link identifies society

↓

Resident verifies phone

↓

Resident enters tower/unit

↓

Membership activated

Use a signed, time-limited token containing:

society_id

invitation_id

expires_at

allowed_use_count

campaign_reference

Do not put a resident’s name, phone or apartment number directly inside the URL.

Recommended status

VERIFIED_BY_INVITE

Method B — Society resident code

The society receives a code such as:

GREENACRES-PET-2026

The resident enters:

Code

Phone verification

Tower

Flat number

This is convenient but weaker because codes may be forwarded outside the society.

Use this for:

PROVISIONAL_VERIFICATION

until additional confirmation occurs.

Method C — Admin or facility approval

Best for high-value services and unclear cases.

Resident submits details

↓

PetSaathi admin reviews request

↓

Authorised society contact confirms residency

↓

Resident becomes verified

The society contact should see only what is necessary:

Resident display name

Tower/block

Unit number

Verification request date

Do not reveal:

Pet medical details

Booking history

Payment details

Emergency contacts

Complaints

Method D — Resident document review

Use only when genuinely necessary.

Possible proof:

Redacted maintenance receipt

Resident card

Rental/occupancy confirmation

Society application screenshot

Approved tenant record

Collect the minimum data needed and allow unnecessary details to be hidden.

The DPDP framework requires PetSaathi to explain what personal data it collects, the purpose of collection and how the person can exercise applicable rights. The final DPDP Rules were published on November 14, 2025 with staged commencement provisions.

Do not permanently store a full document when recording a verification result and limited audit metadata is sufficient.

Recommended verification hierarchy

### Table 33

| Level | Verification | Access |
| --- | --- | --- |
| V0 — Unverified | Phone only | Public services |
| V1 — Provisional | Society code + phone | View resident offers |
| V2 — Verified resident | Invite link or admin confirmation | Book society services |
| V3 — Verified household | Unit ownership/tenancy confirmed | Directory/community features |
| V4 — Society representative | Committee/facility role approved | Limited society administration |

Do not give a society representative access to resident booking or medical information merely because they hold a committee role.

Verification states

NOT_STARTED

PHONE_VERIFIED

DETAILS_SUBMITTED

PROVISIONAL

ADMIN_REVIEW_REQUIRED

VERIFIED

REJECTED

EXPIRED

REVOKED

Reverification triggers

Set verification to review when:

Resident changes society

Tenant leaves

Phone ownership changes

Society reports an invalid unit

Invite link was misused

Verification is older than the configured period

Committee or facility records materially change

Recommended review period:

Owners: every 12–24 months

Tenants: according to lease/occupancy period

Society representatives: every committee term

Verification data model

society_memberships

- id

- society_id

- user_id

- membership_type

- tower

- unit_number_encrypted

- verification_level

- verification_status

- verification_method

- verified_at

- expires_at

- verified_by

- rejection_reason

- revoked_at

- created_at

- updated_at

Membership types:

OWNER

TENANT

FAMILY_MEMBER

AUTHORISED_OCCUPANT

SOCIETY_COMMITTEE

FACILITY_STAFF

SECURITY_STAFF

Invite table

society_invites

- id

- society_id

- token_hash

- invite_type

- max_uses

- used_count

- expires_at

- status

- created_by

- created_at

Store the token hash—not the raw reusable token.

Resident verification API

POST /api/societies/:id/membership/start

POST /api/societies/:id/membership/verify-phone

POST /api/societies/:id/membership/redeem-invite

POST /api/societies/:id/membership/submit

GET /api/me/society-memberships

GET /api/admin/societies/:id/membership-requests

PUT /api/admin/society-memberships/:id/approve

PUT /api/admin/society-memberships/:id/reject

PUT /api/admin/society-memberships/:id/revoke

Every API must verify that the current user can access the exact society-membership object. OWASP identifies manipulation of object IDs as a major API risk and recommends object-level authorization checks whenever an endpoint receives a user-controlled record ID.

Verification-security rules

Rate-limit OTP and invite attempts.

Expire OTPs quickly.

Prevent invite-token reuse beyond its limit.

Encrypt or strongly protect unit numbers.

Never expose complete resident lists publicly.

Do not reveal whether a particular person lives in a society.

Audit approvals, rejections and revocations.

Require elevated permission for bulk verification.

Return generic error messages for invalid resident data.

Never trust a client-sent verification_status.

Resident-facing message

Verify your Green Acres residency to access society-only offers, local sitter availability, community events and resident support. Your verification details are used only to confirm society access and are not shown publicly.

3. Society sitter pool

Goal

Maintain enough trained, verified and locally available caregivers to serve the society without relying on one person.

Your proposed counts are a reasonable starting hypothesis:

### Table 34

| Role | Proposed count |
| --- | --- |
| Primary walkers | 3–5 |
| Backup walkers | 2–3 |
| Pet sitters | 2–5 |
| Boarding hosts nearby | 1–3 |
| Emergency veterinary partners | 1–2 |

However, the final requirement must depend on booking capacity, not only headcount.

Why headcount alone is insufficient

A society may have five walkers, but:

Three may be unavailable in the morning.

One may not handle large dogs.

One may be under safety review.

Two may already have overlapping bookings.

Only one may be registered with security.

The real metric is:

Eligible sitter capacity

for each service window

Recommended launch capacity

Small society

Approximately 10–25 active pet households:

### Table 35

| Role | Recommended |
| --- | --- |
| Primary walkers | 2–3 |
| Backup candidates | 1–2 |
| Pet sitters | 1–2 |
| Boarding partners | 1 controlled |
| Vet coordination partners | 1 primary + 1 fallback |

Medium society

Approximately 25–60 active pet households:

### Table 36

| Role | Recommended |
| --- | --- |
| Primary walkers | 3–5 |
| Backup candidates | 2–3 |
| Pet sitters | 2–4 |
| Boarding partners | 1–2 |
| Vet partners | 1–2 |

Large premium society or township

More than 60 active pet households:

### Table 37

| Role | Recommended |
| --- | --- |
| Primary walkers | 5–10 |
| Backup candidates | 3–5 |
| Pet sitters | 3–6 |
| Boarding partners | 2–3 |
| Vet partners | 2+ |
| Operations coordinator | 1 named owner |

These are initial planning ranges. Actual staffing should be recalculated from demand and sitter utilisation.

Society sitter roles

Primary walker

A primary walker is eligible to receive ordinary confirmed walking assignments.

Requirements:

Identity verification

Dog-walking training

Society access active

Pet-size permission

Required handling skills

Active availability

Acceptable reliability

No safety restriction

Backup walker

A backup walker is not simply “another sitter.”

They must be able to:

Reach the society within the required time

Handle the same pet controls

Work during the same slot

Pass society entry requirements

Avoid schedule conflicts

A candidate list should be refreshed close to service time. An old list does not constitute active backup coverage.

Pet sitter

Home sitting requires additional authorization:

Home-entry training

Key/access procedure

Privacy expectations

Food and medication task permissions

Entry/exit confirmation

Emergency handling

Boarding host

A boarding host must pass a separate process:

Property approval

Capacity limit

Resident-animal information

Safe separation space

Escape prevention

Vaccination/health policy

Compatibility controls

Emergency plan

Society or property permission where required

Veterinary coordination partner

A listed partner may provide:

Clinic contact

Emergency coordination

Vaccination camp

Health-check camp

PetSaathi should label veterinary services as partner-delivered and should not represent itself as the clinical provider.

Society pool states

PENDING_ACCESS

ACTIVE

TEMPORARILY_UNAVAILABLE

CAPACITY_FULL

TRAINING_EXPIRED

VERIFICATION_EXPIRED

SAFETY_REVIEW

SUSPENDED

REVOKED

Only ACTIVE sitters should be considered for automatic candidate generation.

Capacity formula

Calculate available walking capacity by slot:

Available walk capacity =

Eligible active walkers

× safe walks per slot

− existing assigned walks

− reserved backup capacity

Example:

Morning active walkers: 4

Safe bookings per walker: 2

Total capacity: 8

Existing bookings: 6

Reserved backup capacity: 1

New capacity available: 1

Do not continue accepting bookings simply because the society package advertises priority matching.

Dependency protection

No society should depend entirely on one sitter.

Set alerts when:

One sitter handles more than 50% of society bookings

No eligible backup exists

Only one sitter can handle a required pet control

Peak-slot utilisation exceeds 85%

Suggested action:

Recruit another local sitter

Reduce booking cap

Open a waitlist

Alter service windows

Add a paid standby option

Stop promoting the affected slot

Society sitter-pool dashboard

Show administrators:

Active walkers

Active sitters

Backup candidates

Upcoming verification expiry

Upcoming training expiry

Morning capacity

Evening capacity

Current utilisation

Unavailable sitters

Open safety reviews

Single-sitter dependency

The society/security view should be limited to access-relevant fields:

Display name

Photograph

PetSaathi sitter ID

Approved service

Access status

Validity period

PetSaathi support contact

4. Society pet-care calendar

Goal

Create one calendar that helps residents understand:

Available recurring care slots

Society pet events

Booking deadlines

Seasonal pet-safety planning

Package schedules

Partner activities

Do not mix public society events with private individual bookings in the same public view.

Calendar layers

Layer A — Public society events

Visible to residents and, where approved, publicly on the society landing page:

Grooming camp

Vaccination camp

Pet-parent meet-up

Training workshop

Pet first-aid awareness session

Lost-pet preparedness session

Layer B — Verified-resident opportunities

Visible only after resident verification:

Available dog-walking windows

Pet-sitting availability

Society plan enrolment deadlines

Festival care registration

Boarding request deadlines

Resident-exclusive workshops

Layer C — Personal booking calendar

Visible only to the customer:

Confirmed walks

Pet-sitting visits

Boarding check-in/out

Payment deadline

Sitter assignment

Report Card status

Layer D — Operations calendar

Visible to authorised admins:

Sitter shifts

Backup coverage

Event staffing

Capacity blocks

Verification expiries

Partner availability

Incident follow-ups

Dog-walking slots

The calendar may show:

Monday–Friday

6:30–7:00 AM — Limited availability

7:00–7:30 AM — Available

7:30–8:00 AM — Waitlist

5:30–6:00 PM — Available

6:00–6:30 PM — Full

Do not publicly show:

Which resident booked

Pet name

Unit number

Assigned sitter’s full schedule

Exact handover location

A slot means a booking opportunity—not a guaranteed group walk.

Event calendar data

Each public event should include:

Event name

Event type

Society

Date

Start and end time

Venue

Capacity

Registration deadline

Partner

Price

Resident eligibility

Pet requirements

Cancellation status

Google’s Event structured-data documentation recommends providing dedicated event information such as location, start date and event details. Event markup may help qualifying public event pages become eligible for richer search presentation.

Use Event structured data only for genuine public or discoverable event pages—not for private resident bookings.

When an event is postponed, preserve the original schedule in history and notify registered residents.

Holiday and festival planning

Holiday boarding deadlines

Show clear operational deadlines:

Diwali boarding requests

Early registration opens: configured date

Standard deadline: configured date

Late requests: manual review

Final health confirmation: 24–48 hours before check-in

Do not use the same hard-coded date every year. Festival dates and operational capacity must be configured annually.

Diwali pet-safety planning

Recommended calendar content:

Fireworks anxiety preparation session

Medication/veterinary-plan reminder

Quiet-room preparation guide

Boarding/pet-sitting booking deadline

Emergency contact confirmation

Sitter availability notice

Lost-pet prevention reminder

Collar, tag and microchip information reminder

AWBI guidance supports responsible handling of pet dogs in common areas, and its public-parks guidance recognises reasonable walking timings rather than complete exclusion. Society calendar rules should support safe scheduling, not become a tool for blanket pet restrictions.

End-to-end resident journey

Resident visits society page

↓

Selects “I live in this society”

↓

Phone and residency verification

↓

Society membership activated

↓

Resident sees local sitter pool and calendar

↓

↓

What is wrong in the original proposal?

Problem 1 — Flat number is treated as proof

Correction: Use phone verification plus invite, admin approval or another trusted method.

Problem 2 — WhatsApp number is treated as residency proof

Correction: It proves phone control, not physical residence.

Problem 3 — Fixed sitter counts ignore actual capacity

Correction: Measure eligible service capacity by slot, skill and risk controls.

Problem 4 — Backup walkers may also be busy

Correction: Revalidate availability, distance and eligibility when backup is required.

Problem 5 — Calendar could expose private bookings

Correction: Separate public, resident, personal and administrative layers.

Problem 6 — Society calendar may become a restriction tool

Correction: Use it for service availability and reasonable coordination, not blanket pet bans.

Problem 7 — Festival dates may be hard-coded

Correction: Configure exact dates, deadlines and capacity annually.

Definition of done

Resident verification

Phone ownership is verified.

Residency uses a second reliable method.

Membership has an expiry/review process.

Unit details remain protected.

Society representatives receive limited information.

Revoked residents lose private society access.

Sitter pool

Every sitter has active service permission.

Peak-slot capacity is measured.

Backup candidates are genuinely usable.

No society depends entirely on one sitter.

Boarding and veterinary partners have separate approval.

Expiring credentials produce alerts.

Calendar

Public and private calendars are separated.

Capacity and waitlists work.

Rescheduling and cancellation work.

Reminder workflows stop when obsolete.

Festival deadlines are configurable.

Event registration includes appropriate privacy and consent handling.

Private attendee data is not returned through public APIs.

Final operating principle

Verify residents with layered evidence, manage sitters by real service capacity, and use the society calendar to coordinate care and events without exposing private bookings or creating unlawful pet restrictions.

Simple explanation for professor

“The resident-verification feature will confirm that a person genuinely belongs to a society before giving access to resident offers and private society services.

A flat number or WhatsApp number alone is not enough. The user will first verify the phone number and then use a society invite link, resident code or admin approval. Verification can expire or be revoked when the resident moves.

Each society will also have a local sitter pool. A medium society may begin with three to five primary walkers, two or three backup walkers, two to four pet sitters, nearby boarding partners and veterinary contacts. However, the system will measure actual availability and skills instead of relying only on the number of sitters.

The society calendar will show walking windows, grooming and vaccination camps, pet-parent meetings, training workshops and holiday booking deadlines. Public events, private resident opportunities, personal bookings and admin schedules will remain separate.

For Diwali, the calendar may include boarding deadlines, anxiety-preparation reminders, emergency-contact updates and lost-pet-prevention guidance. Dates will be configured for each year rather than hard-coded.

Together, these features ensure that society benefits are protected from misuse, local sitter supply is reliable and residents can easily plan recurring care and pet events.”

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Lost-Pet Alert and Society Pet-Parent Community 🐾🚨🏢

Executive decision

Approve both features with strict boundaries:

### Table 38

| Feature | Decision | Main rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Lost-pet alert | Approve as a controlled emergency feature | Time-limited, verified and privacy-safe |
| Society pet-parent community | Approve with WhatsApp for MVP | Voluntary, moderated and not used for incident management |

The two systems should work together but remain separate:

Lost pet reported

↓

PetSaathi verifies the alert

↓

Structured alert record created

↓

Society security notified

↓

Opted-in residents receive alert

↓

Sightings submitted privately

↓

Pet found or alert expires

The WhatsApp group distributes approved community information. The PetSaathi database remains the authoritative record for alerts, sightings, incidents and resolutions.

5. Lost-pet alert system

Goal

Help reunite an owned pet with its family quickly while protecting:

The owner’s personal information

The resident’s apartment details

The pet from unsafe handling

Society residents from false or excessive notifications

Community animals from being incorrectly labelled as lost pets

Current pet photographs and up-to-date identification are important for recovering missing animals. AVMA recommends keeping identification and microchip information current, while lost-pet guidance from the ASPCA recommends using a recent photograph, physical description, last-seen information and contact details in search materials.

Recommended lost-pet alert information

Public or resident-visible fields

Pet photograph

Pet name

Species

Breed/general description

Colour and distinguishing marks

Sex

Approximate size

Last-seen society area

Last-seen date and time

Safe approach instructions

Alert ID

PetSaathi/admin contact

Optional information

Collar colour

Harness colour

Microchip status: YES / NO / UNKNOWN

Whether the pet is anxious

Whether the pet should be approached

Reward information, after owner confirmation

Do not display publicly

Exact apartment number

Owner’s full private phone number

Pet medical records

Bite-history details

Internal risk classification

Full GPS history

Door-access instructions

Owner emergency contacts

Identity documents

The DPDP Act and final DPDP Rules establish India’s framework for handling digital personal data. The final rules were published on November 14, 2025 with staged commencement provisions, so the alert must collect only necessary information, explain its purpose and restrict who can access sensitive fields.

Important contact correction

The proposal includes:

Owner contact

For safety, do not publish the owner’s direct number by default.

Use one of these:

Call PetSaathi Support

Send a private sighting report

Call society security

Masked owner-contact relay

The owner may explicitly choose to display a contact number, but PetSaathi should explain that residents may forward or screenshot the alert.

Recommended alert contact block

Seen Bruno?Do not chase him. Submit a sighting through PetSaathi or call society security.Alert: LPA-1024

Lost-pet alert activation flow

Step 1 — Owner reports the pet missing

The owner selects:

Report Missing Pet

The system asks:

Which registered pet is missing?

When was the pet last seen?

Where was the pet last seen?

Who last saw the pet?

Is the pet wearing a collar or harness?

Is the pet injured or medically vulnerable?

Is the pet friendly, fearful or likely to run?

Can residents approach the pet?

Should society security be contacted?

Which contact method may PetSaathi use?

Step 2 — Immediate safety instructions

Before alert publication, show:

Search the home and immediate common areas.

Inform society security.

Check stairs, parking areas, gardens and utility spaces.

Keep gates informed.

Do not ask untrained residents to chase a frightened pet.

Contact local shelters, veterinary clinics or appropriate authorities

when the pet may have left the society.

The alert should complement—not replace—local searches, veterinary clinics, shelters or municipal reporting. ASPCA lost-pet guidance recommends checking the immediate area, contacting shelters, veterinary hospitals and local agencies, and sharing consistent recent photographs and descriptions.

Step 3 — Alert verification

Do not instantly broadcast every submitted alert.

Verify:

Reporter owns or is authorised for the pet

Pet Profile exists

Pet photograph is current

Last-seen information is plausible

Society membership is verified

Owner consents to selected information being shared

Alert is not a duplicate

Emergency exception

For an active PetSaathi service where the sitter reports an escape:

Alert status = ACTIVE_EMERGENCY

Incident ticket = created immediately

Society security = notified immediately

Owner = contacted immediately

The normal approval step may happen in parallel.

Step 4 — Create alert

Recommended alert states:

DRAFT

VERIFICATION_REQUIRED

ACTIVE_URGENT

ACTIVE

SIGHTING_REPORTED

SEARCH_AREA_UPDATED

OWNER_CONTACTED

PET_FOUND

EXPIRED

CANCELLED

FALSE_REPORT_REVIEW

ARCHIVED

Priority levels

### Table 39

| Priority | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Critical | Pet in traffic, injured, medically vulnerable or escaped during service |
| High | Pet recently missing inside society |
| Standard | Pet missing for longer period with no immediate hazard |
| Informational | Pet found and owner being located |

Step 5 — Alert distribution

Notify only relevant audiences.

Primary audience

Society security

PetSaathi operations

Verified society residents who opted into alerts

Pet-parent community members

Assigned sitter or active search team

Optional expanded audience

Nearby partner veterinarian

Adjacent PetSaathi societies

Local shelters or animal-support organisations

Nearby opted-in PetSaathi users

Do not send an Ahmedabad alert to all PetSaathi customers across the city.

Geographic notification levels

Level 1 — Same society

Level 2 — Nearby society cluster

Level 3 — Selected locality

Level 4 — Wider city circulation after admin review

Escalation should depend on:

Time missing

Reliable sightings

Whether the pet may have exited the society

Pet mobility

Safety concerns

Owner consent

Sighting-report workflow

Residents should not reply with sightings only in a busy WhatsApp group.

Provide:

Report a Sighting

Ask:

Where did you see the pet?

When?

Was the pet moving in a particular direction?

Did you approach it?

Do you have a photograph?

Can PetSaathi contact you?

Is the pet currently in immediate danger?

Sighting privacy

A sighting should be visible to:

Owner

PetSaathi incident/search administrator

Society security where necessary

Do not publish the reporting resident’s identity to the whole community.

Sighting states

SUBMITTED

UNDER_REVIEW

LIKELY_MATCH

CONFIRMED

NOT_A_MATCH

DUPLICATE

FALSE_REPORT

A single unverified sighting should not automatically change the public last-seen location.

Safe approach instructions

Every alert should contain one of these:

APPROACH_CALMLY

DO_NOT_CHASE

DO_NOT_APPROACH

OFFER_FOOD_AND_NOTIFY

KEEP_VISUAL_CONTACT

CONTACT_SECURITY_IMMEDIATELY

Example:

Bruno may run when frightened. Please do not chase or surround him. Keep him in sight from a safe distance and contact PetSaathi immediately.

Pet found workflow

Possible match located

↓

Owner/pet identity confirmed

↓

Pet condition checked

↓

Veterinary review recommended where necessary

↓

Active alert closed

↓

All alert channels updated

↓

Post-incident review created

Closing notification

Bruno has been found safely. Thank you to Green Acres residents and security for helping. This alert is now closed.

Remove or expire public alert links after closure. Retain only the internal records required for safety, audit and incident review.

Anti-spam rules

Notification limits

Use:

Initial alert

One meaningful search-area update

One confirmed sighting update where useful

Found/closed notification

Do not broadcast:

Every unverified sighting

Repeated “still missing” messages every hour

Emotional speculation

Unverified accusations

Graphic media

Personal disputes

Quiet hours

Critical missing-pet alerts may override quiet hours.

Routine community announcements should follow configured hours.

Recommended lost-pet APIs

POST /api/pets/:id/lost-alert

GET /api/lost-pet-alerts/:code

POST /api/lost-pet-alerts/:code/sightings

PUT /api/lost-pet-alerts/:id/found

PUT /api/lost-pet-alerts/:id/cancel
