PUT /api/admin/lost-pet-alerts/:id/activate

PUT /api/admin/lost-pet-alerts/:id/escalate

PUT /api/admin/lost-pet-alerts/:id/close

Public APIs should return only the approved alert fields, not the complete pet or owner database record.

Prevention feature

The lost-pet system should also encourage prevention:

Current pet photo

Updated phone details

Collar and identification tag

Microchip status

Harness/collar escape history

PetSaathi emergency contact

Safe approach instructions

AVMA recommends keeping identification and microchip information current, and emergency guidance recommends maintaining current pet photographs for identification.

6. Society pet-parent community

Goal

Create a trusted resident communication channel for:

Pet-care announcements

Society events

Local service updates

Sitter introductions

Responsible pet-care education

Lost-pet alerts

Pet-parent networking

Resident feedback

For the Phase 7 MVP, WhatsApp is sufficient, but it should not become PetSaathi’s operational database.

Recommended MVP structure

Instead of one noisy group, use:

A. Announcement group or community

Admins publish:

Service launches

Grooming/vaccination camps

Workshop dates

Walking-plan enrolment

Holiday-care deadlines

Verified lost-pet alerts

Important service updates

Residents do not need to reply to every announcement.

B. Pet-parent discussion group

Residents may discuss:

Pet-friendly local resources

General experiences

Event questions

Walking-plan interest

Community recommendations

This group needs moderation.

C. Private support chat

Use 1:1 PetSaathi WhatsApp support for:

Booking problems

Payments

Refunds

Complaints

Pet Profile questions

Individual sitter issues

Emergencies

D. Formal incident system

Use the PetSaathi platform for:

Injuries

Bites

Missing pets

Unsafe handling

Privacy complaints

Serious service failures

WhatsApp message

↓

Support recognises safety issue

↓

Incident ticket created

↓

Incident ID shared privately

WhatsApp consent

Residents should join voluntarily through:

QR code

Invite link

Resident onboarding form

Community opt-in checkbox

Meta’s WhatsApp Business guidance requires businesses to obtain opt-in before sending business messages to users. PetSaathi should record when, where and for what type of communication the resident opted in.

Suggested consent text

I agree to receive PetSaathi society service updates, event announcements and urgent lost-pet alerts on WhatsApp. I can leave the group or change my communication preferences at any time.

Keep separate preferences for:

Service notifications

Community announcements

Events and offers

Lost-pet emergency alerts

Marketing

Joining an operational alert channel should not automatically create marketing consent.

Important WhatsApp product decision

Meta now documents WhatsApp group capabilities for its Business Platform, but its group functionality has account and onboarding requirements and is not identical to ordinary WhatsApp Business app groups. For the MVP, PetSaathi should use a manually administered WhatsApp group or Community unless there is a clear need and eligibility for API-driven group automation.

Do not delay Phase 7 by building a complex group-management integration.

Community membership rules

Only allow:

Verified society residents

Authorised society contacts

Approved PetSaathi moderators

Selected partner experts during defined events

Do not automatically add:

All society phone numbers

Former residents

Sitters who do not need community access

Sponsors

Groomers or vendors permanently

Unverified outside users

Recommended group name

Green Acres Pet Parents × PetSaathi

Description:

Verified resident community for PetSaathi service updates, pet-care events, responsible pet-parent coordination and approved lost-pet alerts. For booking or emergency support, contact PetSaathi privately.

Community rules

Pin these rules:

Respect all residents and animals.

Do not share apartment numbers, access codes or private contact details.

Do not provide veterinary diagnoses or medication instructions.

Do not publicly accuse a sitter, resident or pet.

Report safety concerns privately to PetSaathi.

Do not post commercial promotions without admin permission.

Use lost-pet alerts only through the verified process.

Do not share graphic or distressing content.

Do not target pets by breed.

Repeated violations may result in removal.

Allowed content

PetSaathi service announcements

Event calendar

Walking-plan availability

Sitter introductions

Pet-care education

Verified lost-pet alerts

Found-pet updates

General pet-parent questions

Society-approved partner events

Restricted content

Home access details

Resident telephone lists

Pet medical records

Unverified sitter complaints

Payment screenshots

Full GPS routes

Risk classifications

Private incident media

Personal conflicts

Unapproved advertising

Moderation roles

PetSaathi moderator

Publishes verified service updates

Handles lost-pet alerts

Removes unsafe content

Redirects complaints

Records significant violations

Escalates incidents

Society representative

Confirms society operational announcements

Helps manage access/event information

Does not access private bookings or Pet Profiles

Veterinary/partner guest

Added only for a scheduled session

Restricted to the relevant topic

Removed after the event when no longer needed

Community escalation model

Normal question

→ Community response

Booking issue

→ Private PetSaathi support

Complaint

→ Complaint ticket

Safety concern

→ Incident ticket

Missing pet

→ Lost-pet alert workflow

Medical emergency

→ Owner + registered veterinarian + incident workflow

Verified service updates

Only post confirmed information.

Examples:

Morning walking slots: Two 7:00–7:30 AM slots are available next week.

Grooming camp: Registration is open for Sunday, August 16. Appointment required.

Service update: Evening walks are temporarily paused due to severe weather. Affected customers are being contacted privately.

Do not share:

Riya has cancelled again because she is unreliable.

Use:

Affected bookings are being reassigned. Customers will receive private updates.

WhatsApp retention and official records

Important operational information should be copied into PetSaathi’s structured system.

What is wrong in the original proposal?

Problem 1 — Publishing the owner’s direct contact

Correction: Use PetSaathi, society security or masked contact by default.

Problem 2 — Every resident can broadcast an alert

Correction: Alerts should be verified before standard distribution.

Problem 3 — A sighting in WhatsApp changes the search area

Correction: Sightings require structured submission and review.

Problem 4 — One group handles everything

Correction: Separate announcements, community discussion, private support and incidents.

Problem 5 — Adding residents without consent

Correction: Residents must voluntarily opt in; business messaging requires prior opt-in.

Problem 6 — WhatsApp becomes the incident database

Correction: Every safety or financial issue must have a structured PetSaathi record.

Problem 7 — Lost-pet alerts could target community animals

Correction: The feature is for verified owned pets. Community-animal matters use separate lawful coordination processes.

Definition of done

Lost-pet alert

Reporter and pet ownership are verified.

A current photograph is available.

Owner contact is protected by default.

Alert contains safe-approach instructions.

Society security is included in the workflow.

Sightings are submitted privately.

Alerts expire or close correctly.

Incident records are created for service-related escapes.

Community animals cannot be falsely processed as owned missing pets.

Society community

Membership is opt-in.

Residents are verified.

Community rules are pinned.

Moderators are assigned.

Announcements and discussion are separated where possible.

Emergencies move to private support.

Complaints and incidents become structured records.

Marketing consent remains separate.

Residents can leave or change notification preferences.

Final operating principle

Use the community to distribute verified information and build trust, but use PetSaathi’s structured systems to manage bookings, sightings, incidents, refunds and private resident data.

Simple explanation for professor

“The lost-pet alert feature will help society residents and security find an owned pet that has gone missing.

The owner will report the pet through PetSaathi and provide a current photograph, the pet’s name, description, last-seen location and safe approach instructions. PetSaathi will verify the report before sending it to residents.

The owner’s apartment number and personal phone number will not be publicly displayed by default. Residents will contact PetSaathi or society security, or submit a private sighting report. When the pet is found, the alert will be closed across all channels.

For the pet-parent community, WhatsApp is sufficient for the MVP. Residents must voluntarily join the community. It can be used for PetSaathi announcements, event information, walking-plan availability, pet-care awareness and verified lost-pet alerts.

However, WhatsApp will not be used as the official incident or booking system. A complaint, injury, refund issue or missing-pet report will always create a structured record inside PetSaathi.

This design allows the community to remain useful and friendly while protecting residents from spam, privacy exposure and unverified information.”

PetSaathi Society Partnership Pitch Deck

Recommended 10-slide structure 🐾🏢

The proposed structure is strong. The deck should tell one simple story:

Society-level pet-care problems

↓

PetSaathi’s organised service model

↓

Benefits for every stakeholder

↓

Safety and operating controls

↓

Low-risk pilot

↓

Evidence and commercial partnership

The deck should not present PetSaathi as a system for controlling pets or enforcing blanket pet restrictions. AWBI publishes specific guidance for pet owners, caregivers, RWAs and apartment-owner associations, so the positioning should focus on responsible coexistence, verified care and organised access.

Slide 1 — The problem

Objective

Show that apartment pet care is not only a pet-parent problem. It also affects security teams, society managers and other residents.

Recommended headline

Pet care inside apartment communities is still fragmented, informal and difficult to manage.

Slide content

Pet parents struggle with

Reliable daily dog walking

Pet care during office hours and travel

Finding screened caregivers

Last-minute cancellations

Receiving trustworthy service updates

Emergency coordination

Societies struggle with

Unknown helpers entering the premises

Unstructured gate approvals

Resident complaints and misunderstandings

No formal escalation process

No reliable list of service providers

Pet-care issues being managed through scattered WhatsApp messages

Better visual

Use a two-column layout:

PET PARENTS SOCIETY OPERATIONS

No reliable walker Unknown service providers

Travel-care stress Gate-access confusion

No service proof Unstructured complaints

Last-minute cancellations stress Gate-access confusion

No service No escalation process

Presenter explanation

“Pet parents need reliable support, but the society also needs controlled access, clear operating rules and a proper complaint process. Today, these needs are usually handled informally.”

Avoid

Do not write:

“Pets create problems in societies.”

“We solve pet menace.”

“We control society pets.”

“We eliminate pet and non-pet conflict.”

Use:

We reduce unmanaged pet-care and access issues through a structured service process.

Slide 2 — The solution

Objective

Explain PetSaathi in one sentence and show the complete resident experience.

Recommended headline

A verified pet-care network designed for apartment communities.

Main statement

PetSaathi connects society residents with approved-access dog walkers, pet sitters and screened boarding partners, supported by service updates, Report Cards and responsive operations.

Core solution blocks

Verified care

Dog walkers

Home pet sitters

Screened boarding beta

Approved specialist partners

Transparent service

Sitter profile and verification indicators

Arrival and service updates

Basic GPS proof for dog walks

Photos and videos

Automated Pet Report Cards

Operational support

Sitter replacement workflow

Structured complaints and incidents

Emergency veterinary coordination

Cancellation and refund support

Suggested process visual

Resident books

↓

Pet Profile reviewed

↓

Eligible local sitter assigned

↓

Payment confirmed

↓

Society entry prepared

↓

Service completed

↓

Report Card delivered

Presenter explanation

“PetSaathi manages the complete workflow—from resident booking and sitter matching to society access, service evidence, Report Cards and issue resolution.”

Important wording

Use:

Emergency veterinary coordination through registered partners

Do not use:

PetSaathi emergency veterinary treatment

Veterinary practice in India is regulated through the Indian Veterinary Council Acterinary practitioners. citeturn393327search12

Slide 3 — Benefits for the entire society

Objective

Demonstrate that the partnership benefits more than pet owners.

Recommended headline

One programme. Benefits for every society stakeholder.

### Table 43

| Stakeholder | Benefit |
| --- | --- |
| Pet parents | Convenient, reliable and documented care |
| RWA/facility manager | Organised service process and fewer unmanaged issues |
| Security team | Approved sitter roster and clear access workflow |
| Non-pet residents | Better service timings, cleanliness expectations and complaint routing |
| Sitters | Verified access and dense local bookings |
| Veterinary/grooming partners | Qualified local demand |
| Society | Stronger resident amenity and community trust |

Stronger benefit line

PetSaathi supports responsible coexistence—not pet control.

Additional operating benefits

Reduced sitter travel time

Faster replacements

Better same-sitter continuity

More recurring walking plans

Lower resident-acquisition cost

Society-level performance visibility

Presenter explanation

“The main advantage is density. When several residents use the service in the same society, sitters travel less, arrive more reliably and can provide repeat care more efficiently.”

Objective

Answer the society committee’s most important question:

“How do you protect residents, pets and the society?”

Recommended headline

Trust is built through layered controls—not one badge.

Suggested six-part safety system

1. Sitter review

Interview

Service permissions

2. Society access

3. Pet Profile

4. Service proof

Basic GPS evidence for walks

Pet Report Card

5. Reliability system

Punctuality

6. Incident support

Structured incident ticket

Severity and escalation

Use a shield in the centre with six surrounding controls:

Verification

Pet Profile

Society access

Service proof

Incident workflow

Emergency coordination

Presenter explanation

“We do not claim zero risk. We reduce operational risk through verification, Pet Profiles, controlled matching, service evidence and a documented escalation process.”

Claims to avoid

“100% safe”

“Completely risk-free”

“Guaranteed trusted”

“Zero incidents”

“Police-approved platform”

Advertising and pitch claims should be factual, honest and supportable; ASCI’s code specifically emphasises truthful represenainst misleading claims. citeturn393327search6

Privacy note

Residents should register through an opt-in form. The society should not simply give PetSaathi its complete resident contact list.

India’s DPDP Act recognises the protection of digital personal data, and the final DPDP Rules were published on November 14, 2025 with staged commencement. The deck should therefore promise voluntary registration, purpose-liting. citeturn393327search2turn393327search11

Recommended deck narrative

The deck should flow emotionally and logically:

Slide 1:

There is an unmanaged operational problem.

Slide 2:

PetSaathi has an organised solution.

Slide 3:

Every stakeholder benefits.

Slide 4:

Safety and verification are built into the process.

Slide 5:

The society receives useful recurring services.

Slide 6:

The first step is low risk.

Slide 7:

There is a clear long-term partnership model.

Slide 8:

Implementation is simple.

Slide 9:

Results are measurable.

Slide 10:

Approve the pilot.

Services are subject to Pet Profile review, sitter eligibility, availability and applicable society procedures. Veterinary services are delivered through qualified partners. PetSaathi does not guarantee zero risk.

Pitch delivery length

Five-minute society pitch

### Table 48

| Section | Time |
| --- | --- |
| Problem | 35 seconds |
| Solution | 40 seconds |
| Stakeholder benefits | 35 seconds |
| Safety | 50 seconds |
| Services | 35 seconds |
| Pilot | 45 seconds |
| Packages | 30 seconds |
| Launch process | 30 seconds |
| Evidence | 30 seconds |
| CTA | 20 seconds |

Two-minute summary

“PetSaathi provides verified pet-care support for apartment communities. Residents can book dog walkers and home pet sitters through a structured platform with Pet Profiles, approved-access caregivers, payment verification, service updates and Pet Report Cards.

The society benefits from a controlled sitter roster, clearer entry procedures, a formal complaint process and better coordination between pet parents, security and management. We can also coordinate screened boarding, partner-led grooming or vaccination camps and emergency veterinary contacts.

We recommend beginning with a controlled 30-day pilot. There is no society setup fee during the pilot; residents pay for only the services they book. PetSaathi manages onboarding, sitter access, WhatsApp support, trial bookings and an aggregate performance report.

At the end of the pilot, we review paid bookings, punctuality, resident ratings, repeat demand, entry issues and complaints. The society can then decide whether to continue with a Bsic Partner or Premium Pet-Care Desk programme.”

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Society Outreach System 🏢🐾

Executive decision

Your channel list is strong, but the outreach system should not treat every channel equally.

The most effective sequence is:

Existing pet parent or local partner introduction

↓

RWA/facility-manager conversation

↓

Resident-demand validation

↓

15-minute discovery meeting

↓

Society pilot proposal

↓

Security and access discussion

↓

Resident opt-in campaign

↓

Controlled pilot launch

WhatsApp should be the primary follow-up and conversion channel, not the default cold-prospecting channel. Meta requires businesses to obtain opt-in before messaging people on WhatsApp. For SMS and commercial calls, TRAI’s framework similarly centres commercial communications on customer consent and registered sender processes.

1. Society outreach channel priorities

Priority 1 — Warm introductions and resident-led access

These should generate the highest-quality conversations.

Pet-parent groups

Purpose: Prove resident demand before approaching the committee.

A society committee is more likely to consider the proposal when several residents already want:

Daily walking support

Travel-time sitting

Same-sitter plans

Festival care

Grooming or veterinary-partner camps

Recommended process

Existing resident expresses interest

↓

Resident introduces PetSaathi to pet-parent group admin

↓

Admin approves one information post

↓

Interested residents opt in

↓

PetSaathi measures demand

↓

Resident champion introduces RWA/facility manager

Do not add residents to a group or contact them individually from a society list without an appropriate opt-in. Under India’s current data-protection framework, PetSaathi should explain what information is collected, why it is needed and how it will be used.

Best lead indicator

10+ residents interested

or

5+ residents ready for a paid trial

A WhatsApp group with 200 members but only one potential customer is not meaningful demand.

Priority 2 — Direct B2B decision-makers

RWA or managing committee

Role: Commercial and policy approval.

Discuss:

Resident benefit

Approved sitter access

Pilot duration

Data-sharing limits

Complaint escalation

Society reporting

Commercial package after pilot

The committee pitch should focus on organised pet-care access and coexistence, not controlling or restricting pets.

Best first contact

Warm resident introduction

Email or LinkedIn introduction

Short phone call

Permission-based WhatsApp follow-up

Formal meeting

Desired outcome

Not an immediate contract. The first outcome should be:

A 15-minute society discovery meeting.

Facility manager

Role: Operational champion.

Facility managers usually understand:

Visitor access

Security procedures

Tower and gate complexity

Existing resident complaints

Event permissions

Common-area limitations

Committee approval processes

The facility manager may not have authority to sign the partnership, but can determine whether the programme is operationally possible.

Questions to ask

How are external service providers currently registered?

Does security require advance approval?

Are there permitted service hours?

Is there an active pet-parent group?

How many towers and gates are involved?

Who can formally approve a pilot?

Are resident events permitted?

What complaints commonly occur?

Security office

Role: Access-design stakeholder, not initial sales target.

Security should be engaged after the society or facility manager shows interest.

Discuss:

Sitter photo and PetSaathi ID

Access validity

Booking confirmation

Entry and exit procedures

Replacement sitter approval

Emergency escalation

Revoked sitter access

Do not expect the security team to approve the commercial pilot.

2. Local trust and referral channels

Local veterinarians

Purpose

Veterinary clinics can:

Introduce PetSaathi to pet-heavy societies

Participate in health or vaccination camps

Provide emergency-coordination contacts

Refer busy pet parents

Strengthen local credibility

Recommended partnership request

Do not begin by asking:

“Can you promote PetSaathi to all customers?”

Use:

“We are building verified society-level pet-care support. We would like to explore resident education, referral and veterinary-partner coordination in nearby apartment communities.”

Referral tracking

Record:

Partner

Society referred

Introduction date

Meeting completed

Pilot signed

Paid residents acquired

Referral fee or commercial terms

Medical recommendations must remain under the control of qualified veterinary professionals; PetSaathi should describe veterinary services as partner-delivered coordination rather than its own clinical care.

Pet shops and groomers

Purpose

Pet shops often know:

High-pet-density societies

Active pet-parent groups

Frequently travelling customers

Local grooming demand

Communities interested in events

Partnership options

Society introduction

QR referral cards

Grooming camp

Co-hosted awareness event

Resident offer

Fixed converted-customer referral fee

Avoid exchanging full customer lists. Use resident-controlled QR forms or referral links.

Dog parks and walking areas

Purpose

Use them for market observation and voluntary conversations, not aggressive selling.

Learn:

Which societies have many dog owners

Popular walking times

Common pet-care problems

Existing walker networks

Society clusters with high demand

Good field approach

“We are researching verified pet-care support for nearby apartment communities. Which societies in this area have active pet-parent groups?”

Do not photograph, record or collect resident information without permission.

3. Digital outreach channels

LinkedIn

Best targets

Facility managers

Property managers

Community managers

RWA office bearers with public professional profiles

Real-estate developer experience teams

Premium property-management companies

Correct method

Use highly personalised outreach:

Name

Society/property

Area

Relevant operational problem

One-sentence PetSaathi solution

15-minute meeting request

Do not use automated mass connection requests. LinkedIn states that excessive invitations, ignored invitations, spam reports or suspected automation can lead to account restrictions; it recommends relevant, thoughtful invitations.

Suggested cadence

Day 1: Personalised connection request

Day 3–4: Short value message after acceptance

Day 7: Share one-page pilot summary

Day 12: Final polite follow-up

Then stop

Facebook groups

Best groups

Local apartment-community groups

Area pet-parent groups

City dog-owner groups

Resident community groups

Local lost-and-found pet groups

Correct approach

Ask the administrator before promotional posting.

Post useful content first:

Society pet-care checklist

Festival pet-safety guide

How to verify a dog walker

Travel-care preparation

Lost-pet prevention

Meta allows Pages to participate in or create groups, but group administrators and members can moderate or report spam.

Avoid

Posting identical advertisements in dozens of groups

Repeatedly commenting “DM us”

Messaging group members privately without invitation

Claiming unverified society partnerships

Using resident posts to build hidden contact lists

WhatsApp

Best use

WhatsApp is ideal for:

Warm introductions

Permission-based follow-up

Resident interest collection

Meeting confirmation

Pilot updates

Booking assistance

Event registration

Service notifications

Meta requires an opt-in before a business initiates WhatsApp communication. Marketing messages and utility messages also serve different purposes: marketing templates promote offers, while utility templates generally follow a user action or request.

Correct acquisition flow

Poster / resident group / referral

↓

Resident clicks WhatsApp link

↓

Resident sends “PET”

↓

Opt-in and interest recorded

↓

PetSaathi shares details

↓

Resident books or joins waitlist

Incorrect flow

Society shares 300 resident phone numbers

↓

PetSaathi sends bulk promotional messages

TRAI treats unwanted commercial messages and calls as unsolicited commercial communications and provides complaint/reporting mechanisms.

4. Recommended channel allocation

### Table 49

| Channel | Primary purpose | Priority |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Existing pet parents | Warm society introductions | P0 |

5. End-to-end outreach workflow

Step 1 — Build a qualified society list

For each society, record:

Society name

Area

Approximate homes

Estimated pet-parent density

Facility manager

RWA/committee contact

Existing resident champion

Security complexity

Nearby sitter capacity

Nearby veterinary partner

Lead source

Do not purchase or scrape private resident contact lists.

Step 2 — Score every society

Recommended score:

### Table 50

| Factor | Weight |
| --- | --- |
| Resident pet demand | 25% |
| Local sitter supply | 20% |
| Geographic fit | 15% |
| Committee accessibility | 15% |
| Security feasibility | 10% |
| Society size | 10% |
| Nearby partner ecosystem | 5% |

Priority classification:

80–100: Priority target

60–79: Qualified target

40–59: Nurture

Below 40: Do not actively pursue

Step 3 — Identify three contacts

For every target society, find:

Resident champion

Operational contact

Decision-maker

Example:

Resident champion: Pet-parent group administrator

Operational contact: Facility manager

Decision-maker: RWA secretary

This is stronger than relying on one person.

Step 4 — Send the first message

The first message should:

Identify the sender

Explain the local relevance

Present one clear benefit

Request only 15 minutes

Avoid sending large attachments

Avoid unsupported claims

ASCI’s current code requires objectively verifiable advertising claims to be capable of substantiation. Therefore, use “screened,” “identity reviewed” or “trained” only where PetSaathi actually performs and records those checks.

Step 5 — Follow-up cadence

B2B prospect

Day 1: First introduction

Day 3: Short follow-up

Day 7: Send one-page pilot summary

Day 12: Final follow-up

Day 30: Re-engage only with meaningful new information

Stop following up when:

The person declines

They request no further messages

The society is not interested

Contact details appear incorrect

Multiple messages receive no response

Step 6 — Conduct a discovery call

15-minute agenda

Minutes 0–3:

Society overview and current pet-care situation

Minutes 3–7:

Resident demand and common problems

Minutes 7–10:

Security and access process

Minutes 10–13:

PetSaathi pilot model

Minutes 13–15:

Decision process and next step

Discovery questions

How many homes are occupied?

Is there an active pet-parent group?

What are the most common pet-care needs?

How are outside walkers currently admitted?

Are there common security or cleanliness issues?

Which service timings are most useful?

Who must approve a pilot?

Would the society distribute an opt-in resident survey?

Step 7 — Validate resident demand

Use a short opt-in survey:

Name

Society/tower

Phone

Pet type

Interested service

Preferred time

Trial interest

Consent to receive PetSaathi information

Do not ask for medical or behavioural information during lead collection. Collect that later through the private Pet Profile.

Step 8 — Present the pilot

Recommended pilot:

Duration: 21–30 days

Society setup fee: ₹0

Partnership fee: ₹0

Residents pay for booked services

Initial services: Walking + sitting

Booking cap: Based on sitter capacity

Monthly aggregate summary: Included

Automatic renewal: No

Step 9 — Configure and launch

Pilot approved

↓

Society record created

↓

Gate procedures documented

↓

Local sitter roster approved

↓

Resident announcement released

↓

Registrations opened

↓

First bookings monitored

↓

Pilot review

Track:

Meetings

Revenue

A channel producing many contacts but no paid bookings should be deprioritised.

8. Corrected society outreach message

Your original message is useful, but it makes three claims too broadly:

“safe boarding” should be screened or controlled boarding beta

Not every service necessarily contains video updates

Veterinary care should be described as coordination through qualified partners

Here is the stronger version:

Hi [Name],

I’m [Your Name] from PetSaathi. We are launching a structured pet-care support programme for apartment communities in [Area].

PetSaathi helps residents book:

• screened dog walkers• in-home pet sitters• controlled boarding, where available• emergency veterinary coordination through registered partners• partner-led grooming and vaccination camps

Our service workflow includes sitter screening, society-access coordination, booking updates and a Pet Report Card after completed services. Dog walks may also include basic GPS and photo proof.

We would like to offer [Society Name] a controlled 30-day pilot with no society setup fee. Residents would pay only for the services they choose to book.

Would you be available for a 15-minute discussion on [Option 1] or [Option 2]?

Regards,[Your Name]PetSaathi[Phone / WhatsApp][Website]

9. Corrected pet-parent group message

The group message should be posted by the group administrator or with their permission. “Reply PET” creates a clear expression of interest, after which PetSaathi can respond privately.

Hi everyone 🐾

PetSaathi is planning a verified pet-care pilot for residents of [Society/Area].

Available services may include:

• 30-minute and 60-minute dog walks• in-home pet sitting• screened boarding beta• emergency veterinary coordination through registered partners

Residents receive booking updates and a Pet Report Card after completed services. Dog walks may also include basic GPS and photo proof.

🎉 Launch offer: First eligible 30-minute dog walk from ₹99, subject to sitter availability, Pet Profile review and pilot capacity.

Reply PET or use this link to receive the service details privately: [Opt-in Link]

Your contact information will be used only to share PetSaathi pilot and booking information. You may opt out at any time.

10. Short first-contact version

Use this for LinkedIn or a warm WhatsApp introduction:

Hi [Name], I’m [Your Name] from PetSaathi.

We help apartment communities organise verified dog-walking and pet-sitting support with approved sitter access, service updates, Pet Report Cards and a structured issue-escalation process.

We are selecting a small number of societies in [Area] for a 30-day pilot with no society setup fee.

Would a 15-minute introductory discussion be possible this week?

These messages can be shortened further for specific surfaces, but the legal and operational qualifications should remain intact.

11. Recommended outreach assets

Prepare these before contacting societies:

Ten-slide society pitch deck

One-page pilot summary

Society FAQ

Resident interest form

Sitter verification explainer

Sample Pet Report Card

Society access workflow

Pilot pricing sheet

Privacy and data-sharing summary

Calendar-booking link

Do not send all ten assets in the first message. Use:

First message:

Short introduction

After interest:

One-page summary

Before meeting:

Pitch deck

After meeting:

Custom pilot proposal

12. Common outreach mistakes

Mistake 1 — Cold WhatsApp blasting

Problem: Low trust, spam reports and weak consent.

Correction: Use introductions, public opt-in links and permission-based follow-up. Meta requires business messaging opt-in, while TRAI provides mechanisms to report unwanted commercial communications.

Mistake 2 — Selling to security first

Problem: Security rarely has commercial authority.

Correction: Get society or facility approval first, then design the access process with security.

Mistake 3 — Starting with every service

Problem: The pitch becomes complex.

Correction: Lead with dog walking and pet sitting. Present boarding and partner camps as controlled secondary offerings.

Mistake 4 — Promising “verified” without defining it

Correction: Explain whether verification includes identity review, interview, training, society access or other checks.

Mistake 5 — Asking the society for its resident database

Correction: Ask the society to distribute a resident-controlled opt-in form.

Mistake 6 — No clear CTA

Correction: Request a 15-minute discovery call, not a vague “let us know.”

Mistake 7 — Repeated follow-ups

Correction: Use a limited cadence and respect decline or silence.

Mistake 8 — Measuring only replies

Correction: Measure pilot approvals, paid bookings, repeat services and contribution margin.

Final operating principle

Use residents and trusted local partners to open doors, use committees and facility managers to approve the programme, use security to operationalise access, and use permission-based WhatsApp communication to convert interested residents into bookings.

Simple explanation for professor

“PetSaathi will use different outreach channels for different purposes.

Pet parents and local veterinary or pet-shop partners can introduce us to societies where real demand already exists. The RWA or committee will make the partnership decision, while the facility manager will help us understand service timings, security procedures and common resident concerns. The security office will be involved after the society shows interest so that sitter entry and replacement rules can be configured.

LinkedIn will be used for personalised contact with facility and property managers. Facebook groups and dog parks will mainly help us identify pet-parent communities and society clusters. WhatsApp will be the main follow-up and resident-conversion channel, but residents must voluntarily request information or opt in.

The first outreach message will briefly explain PetSaathi and request a 15-minute meeting. After a society responds, we will conduct a discovery call, run a resident-interest survey and present a controlled 30-day pilot.

The society should not give PetSaathi its full resident contact list. Residents will register themselves through a QR code or opt-in form.

The success of outreach will not be measured only by messages or meetings. We will track signed pilots, paid resident bookings, repeat walking plans, customer ratings and the contribution generated by each society.”

A monthly compliance watch for WhatsApp, TRAI, privacy and advertising changes would help keep these outreach templates current.

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Week 1: Society Partnership Preparation 🏢🐾

Week 1 objective

By the end of Day 7, PetSaathi should possess a complete society-sales and pilot-launch kit that allows the team to:

Identify a suitable society

↓

Contact the decision-maker

↓

Explain the business model

↓

Validate resident demand

↓

Send a formal pilot proposal

↓

Sign a pilot agreement

↓

Configure the society page

↓

Begin resident onboarding

Week 1 is not for approaching hundreds of societies immediately. It is for creating one consistent system so that pricing, safety claims, resident privacy, operations and contract terms do not change from one sales conversation to another.

Recommended Week 1 sequence

### Table 53

| Day | Task | Primary output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Day 1 | Finalise society offer | Approved packages and commercial rules |
| Day 2 | Create pitch deck | 7–10 slide presentation and PDF |
| Day 3 | Build landing-page template | Reusable society page |
| Day 4 | Prepare outreach scripts | WhatsApp, email, call and follow-up templates |
| Day 5 | Configure society CRM | Lead pipeline and reporting sheet |
| Day 6 | Draft pilot agreement | Legal and operational pilot document |
| Day 7 | Review all launch material | Outreach-ready partnership kit |

Day 1 — Finalise the society offer

Goal

Create one approved commercial and operational framework that tells the team:

Which packages exist

Who each package is for

What is included

What remains excluded

Who pays

What residents pay separately

What PetSaathi promises

What PetSaathi does not promise

Step 1: Approve the four-package ladder

Package 1 — No-Fee Society Pilot

Best for: first-time society testing.

Society setup fee: ₹0

Pilot partnership fee: ₹0

Duration: 15–30 days

Recommended default: 21–30 days

Resident care services: Paid

Automatic renewal: No

Included:

Society onboarding

Resident trial offer

Approved-access sitter roster

WhatsApp booking assistance

Awareness poster

Society landing page

Aggregate pilot report

Package 2 — Basic Society Partner

Best for: societies that have demonstrated real resident demand.

Price:

₹5,000–₹15,000 per month

+ applicable taxes

Included:

Society-specific landing page

Resident benefits

Priority matching among eligible sitters

Monthly aggregate report

Dedicated WhatsApp routing

Named operations owner

Society access roster

Package 3 — Premium Pet-Care Desk

Best for: larger premium societies and townships.

Price:

₹15,000–₹50,000 per month

+ applicable taxes

Included:

Named coordinator

Regular walking windows

Enhanced sitter and backup pool

Partner-led camps

Society events

Lost-pet alert system

Safety reporting

Priority escalation

A full-time exclusive on-site coordinator should remain a separately priced staffing arrangement.

Package 4 — Event and Camp Programme

Best for: lead generation and resident activation.

Possible events:

Veterinary-partner health-check camp

Registered-veterinarian vaccination camp

Grooming camp

Pet-parent meetup

Humane training workshop

Revenue may come from:

Event coordination fee

+

Transparent ticket income

+

Sponsor contribution

+

Defined partner revenue share

+

Future care bookings

Step 2: Approve the positioning

Official positioning

PetSaathi helps apartment communities organise verified pet-care support through approved-access dog walkers, in-home pet sitters, screened boarding partners, service updates, Report Cards and structured issue escalation.

Avoid

“We manage all pets in your society.”

“We solve the pet menace.”

“Completely safe pet care.”

“Guaranteed trusted sitters.”

“Zero-risk care.”

AWBI’s published guidance addresses pet owners, caregivers, RWAs and apartment-owner associations and states that RWAs and AOAs should not impose blanket bans on keeping pet dogs. PetSaathi should therefore sell responsible service coordination and coexistence—not pet control.

ASCI’s advertising code requires claims to be truthful, supportable and not misleading through wording or omission. Terms such as “verified,” “safe,” “free” and “guaranteed” must therefore be precisely defined.

Step 3: Define package boundaries

Create an inclusion matrix:

### Table 54

| Capability | Pilot | Basic | Premium | Event |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Society landing page | Yes | Yes | Yes | Event page |
| Resident booking support | Basic | Priority | Enhanced | Event-specific |
| Approved sitter roster | Yes | Yes | Enhanced | As needed |
| Monthly report | Pilot summary | Yes | Detailed | Event report |
| Coordinator | Shared operations | Named owner | Named coordinator | Event lead |
| Resident events | No/optional | Limited | Included by tier | Core |
| Veterinary services | Coordination | Coordination | Partner-led | Partner-led |
| Guaranteed sitter | No | No | No | No |
| Resident care included in fee | No | No | No | Only if contract states |
| Private resident data for society | No | No | No | No |

Step 4: Finalise pricing rules

Every package should state:

Society fee

Applicable taxes

Resident booking charges

Discount funding

Partner charges

Event charges

Refund responsibility

Renewal terms

Termination notice

Digital service pages and checkouts should clearly communicate service identity, prices, payment terms, refund terms and grievance mechanisms. India’s Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules apply to services sold digitally and contain disclosure and grievance-related obligations.

Day 1 output

Create a document named:

PetSaathi_Society_Package_Matrix_v1

It should contain:

Positioning statement

Package comparison

Pricing

Included and excluded features

Support hours

Resident charges

Commercial assumptions

Safety disclaimers

Data-sharing restrictions

Approval owner

Day 1 definition of done

All packages have exact names.

“Free” clearly means no society partnership fee—not free resident care.

“Priority” does not mean guaranteed availability.

Veterinary services are described as partner-led coordination.

Pricing and taxes are clearly stated.

Package limits are measurable.

The founder, operations lead and finance owner approve the final matrix.

Day 2 — Create the society pitch deck

Goal

Produce a persuasive presentation that a committee or facility manager can understand within five to ten minutes.

Recommended 10-slide structure

Slide 1 — Society problem

Cover:

Unverified helpers

Inconsistent dog walking

Travel-related pet-care needs

Gate-entry confusion

Scattered complaints

Slide 2 — PetSaathi solution

→ Pet Profile

Slide 3 — Stakeholder benefits

Slide 4 — Safety and verification

Slide 5 — Services

Prioritise:

Dog walking

In-home sitting

Emergency veterinary coordination

Slide 6 — Pilot offer

Explain:

30-day controlled pilot

No society setup fee

Residents pay for booked services

Resident support

Aggregate report

Slide 7 — Packages

Slide 8 — Launch process

Approval

→ Access setup

→ Resident announcement

→ Registrations

→ Sitter mapping

→ Trial bookings

→ Pilot report

Slide 9 — Evidence

Use only verified PetSaathi data.

Include:

When actual data is unavailable, label the slide:

Slide 10 — Call to action

Pitch-deck quality rules

One idea per slide

Maximum five or six short points

Large, readable typography

Real or licensed imagery

No invented customer metrics

No unsupported “100% safe” claims

Pricing marked indicative when not final

Strong CTA and contact details

PetSaathi_5_Minute_Pitch_Notes

Day 2 definition of done

Deck can be delivered in under seven minutes.

Claims match actual product functionality.

Pricing matches Day 1.

Pilot terms match the agreement draft.

No fake metrics or endorsements appear.

The PDF is readable on mobile.

The final slide contains one clear action.

Day 3 — Create the reusable society landing-page template

Goal

Build one technical template that can generate accurate society pages without manually redesigning each page.

Recommended route

/pet-care/societies/[city]/[societySlug]

Example:

/pet-care/societies/ahmedabad/green-acres-bopal

Next.js App Router supports dynamic route segments such as [city] and [societySlug]. Dynamic metadata can also be generated from route-specific data using generateMetadata.

Recommended page sections

Society identity

Active partnership status

Available services

Resident offer

Booking CTA

Local sitter summaries

How booking works

Safety controls

Society access information

Report Card preview

Emergency coordination

FAQs

Partner veterinarian/groomer

Privacy and policy links

Data-driven architecture

society

services

resident offer

active sitter summaries

access rules

partners

FAQs

programme metrics

support information

Do not hard-code society names and details inside the React components.

Public-page restrictions

Do not expose:

Resident names

Flat numbers

Private phone numbers

Gate codes

Pet medical information

Full GPS routes

KYC documents

Internal complaint data

The DPDP Rules, 2025 were published on November 14, 2025 with staggered commencement dates. Landing-page registration and lead forms should therefore be designed with clear notices, defined purposes, restricted access and controlled retention from the beginning.

SEO publication rules

Use page states:

DRAFT

PILOT

ACTIVE

PAUSED

ENDED

ARCHIVED

Only complete and genuinely active society pages should normally be indexable.

Google treats large numbers of substantially similar location pages created mainly to rank for related searches as potential doorway or scaled-content abuse. Every indexed society page should contain authentic local services, availability, access rules, FAQs and operating information.

Day 3 output

Create:

SocietyLandingPage

SocietyServiceCard

SocietySitterCard

ResidentOfferCard

SocietyTrustSection

SocietyFAQ

SocietyPartnerCard

SocietyBookingCTA

And a sample page for one fictional or approved pilot society.

Day 3 definition of done

Society data is loaded dynamically.

Inactive services cannot be booked.

The society ID is attached to the booking.

Private data never enters the public response.

Draft pages use noindex.

Metadata is society-specific.

The page is responsive and mobile-first.

The CTA works from page to booking flow.

Day 4 — Create outreach scripts

Goal

Give every team member approved language for contacting societies and residents.

Required script library

1. RWA/committee message

Purpose:

Explain the local problem

Introduce PetSaathi

Offer a pilot

Request a 15-minute call

2. Facility-manager message

Focus on:

Service-provider access

Gate procedures

Walking timings

Resident support

Operational reporting

3. Resident champion message

Ask for:

Pet-parent demand

Introduction to the group admin

Introduction to committee/facility contact

4. Veterinarian/pet-shop message

Focus on:

Society introductions

Partner-led events

Referral arrangements

Local pet-care ecosystem

5. Pet-parent group announcement

Include:

Available services

Trial offer

Opt-in link

Privacy notice

Capacity limits

6. Email introduction

Use a more formal version with:

Problem

Solution

Pilot summary

Meeting request

One-page attachment

7. Follow-up messages

Prepare:

Day 3 follow-up

Day 7 value follow-up

Day 12 final polite follow-up

WhatsApp compliance rule

Businesses must obtain user opt-in before sending WhatsApp business messages, and messages should be relevant to the permission provided. Society residents should therefore join through an invite, QR link or resident-initiated conversation rather than being imported into a promotional list.

Script claims checklist

Every script should answer:

What does “verified” mean?

Is boarding active or beta?

Are veterinary services partner-led?

Are residents paying?

Is sitter availability guaranteed?

Is the offer limited?

How can recipients opt out?

Day 4 output

Create:

PetSaathi_Society_Outreach_Script_Library_v1

Containing:

Four WhatsApp scripts

Two email templates

One phone-call opener

One discovery-call agenda

Three follow-up messages

One resident group announcement

One opt-out response

One rejection/nurture response

Day 4 definition of done

Messages are personalised, not spam-like.

Resident outreach requires opt-in.

No unsupported safety promises appear.

The CTA asks for one clear next step.

Boarding and veterinary claims are accurate.

Follow-up stops after rejection or opt-out.

All team members use the same approved wording.

Day 6 — Create the pilot agreement draft

Goal

Prepare a short but operationally complete agreement for legal review and society negotiation.

This is not merely a sales proposal. It must clearly allocate obligations between:

PetSaathi

Society/RWA/AOA

Residents

Sitters

Professional partners

Under the Indian Contract Act, agreements become contracts when legal requirements such as competent parties, free consent, lawful consideration and lawful object are satisfied. The draft should be reviewed by a qualified Indian lawyer before use across different society and state structures.

Recommended agreement sections

1. Parties

PetSaathi legal entity

Society/RWA/AOA legal name

Registered address

Authorised representative

Effective date

2. Purpose

Controlled resident pet-care pilot within the named society.

3. Duration

Start date

End date

No automatic renewal

Extension process

4. Pilot services

Specify exactly:

Dog walking

In-home sitting

Controlled boarding, if applicable

Resident support

Partner events, if applicable

5. Commercial terms

Society fee

Resident service pricing

Taxes

Discounts

Payment responsibility

Refund responsibility

Partner charges

6. PetSaathi responsibilities

Maintain sitter approval records

Control assignment

Process bookings/payment

Provide service updates

Manage complaints/incidents

Protect resident information

Provide aggregate report

7. Society responsibilities

Nominate authorised contacts

Share approved resident announcement

Define gate procedure

Support approved sitter access

Notify changes

Avoid unauthorised resident-data sharing

Route complaints through the agreed channel

8. Resident relationship

State that:

Residents register voluntarily.

Residents provide accurate Pet Profiles.

Individual service contracts/payments remain separate unless expressly agreed.

The society does not guarantee individual services.

9. Sitter access

Define:

Registration

Photo/ID display

Validity

Replacement access

Revocation

Emergency entry

10. Safety and incidents

Include:

Incident reporting

Emergency contacts

Cooperation

Evidence preservation

No guarantee of zero risk

Authority to pause services

11. Veterinary and specialist partners

State that clinical or specialist services are delivered by appropriately qualified partners and not by ordinary PetSaathi support staff.

12. Data protection

Cover:

Data collected

Purpose

Who can access it

Society reporting

Retention

Security

Incident notification

Resident requests

13. Brand usage

Society-name/logo permission

PetSaathi branding

Testimonials

Photography

Public announcements

14. Liability and indemnity

This section requires lawyer-led drafting based on the legal entity, insurance and state-specific society structure.

15. Termination

Include:

Notice period

Immediate suspension reasons

Safety obstruction

Non-payment

Data misuse

Illegal demands

Unauthorised branding

Repeated sitter-access refusal

16. Disputes and governing law

Require legal advice for:

Jurisdiction

Arbitration

Notice

Escalation

Governing law

Parties and authorised signatories are clear.

Week 1 readiness scorecard

### Table 56

| Requirement | Pass condition |
| --- | --- |
| Society packages | Approved and priced |
| Pitch deck | Mobile-readable and factually accurate |
| Landing page | Working reusable template |
| Outreach scripts | Approved and opt-in compliant |
| CRM | Pipeline and next actions functional |
| Pilot agreement | Complete draft sent for legal review |
| FAQ | Covers society, security and resident concerns |
| Claims | Supported and consistently worded |
| Privacy | Notices and access rules defined |
| Pilot operations | End-to-end tabletop test passed |

Common Week 1 mistakes

Mistake 1 — Designing the deck before finalising the offer

This creates inconsistent pricing and promises.

Correct order:

Offer

→ Deck

→ Page

→ Scripts

→ Agreement

Mistake 2 — Treating the landing page as only a design task

It must represent real services, capacity, privacy and booking states.

Mistake 3 — Building CRM with no next-action field

A contact list is not a CRM.

Every opportunity needs:

Owner

Stage

Next action

Next date

Mistake 4 — Copying outreach scripts for every society

Personalise:

Society name

Area

Likely resident need

Relevant service

Lead source

Mistake 5 — Launching before the agreement and SOP agree

The sales deck must not promise anything operations cannot deliver.

Mistake 6 — Calling the pilot entirely free

Correct wording:

No society setup or partnership fee; residents pay for booked services.

Mistake 7 — Treating the agreement as a downloaded generic template

The agreement must reflect PetSaathi’s real booking, access, payment, privacy, incident and partner workflows.

Final Week 1 operating principle

Every society should receive the same accurate commercial offer, safety explanation, privacy standard and pilot process—even when the pitch and landing page are personalised to that society.

Simple explanation for professor

“During the first week of Phase 7, we will prepare everything required before approaching apartment societies.

On Day 1, we will finalise our four society packages, prices, included features and limitations.

On Day 2, we will create a professional pitch deck explaining the society problem, PetSaathi’s solution, safety process, services, pilot offer, packages and launch process.

On Day 3, we will create a reusable society landing-page template. Each active society can have a personalised page showing available services, local sitters, resident offers, booking options, FAQs and support information.

On Day 4, we will prepare approved WhatsApp, email, phone and follow-up scripts. Residents will receive messages only after voluntarily requesting information or opting in.

On Day 5, we will create a CRM to track every society, contact, meeting, proposal, next action and pilot result.

On Day 6, we will prepare a pilot agreement covering services, payment, sitter access, society responsibilities, resident privacy, incidents, branding and termination. A legal professional should review it before use.

On Day 7, the product, operations, sales, finance, safety and legal teams will review all materials together. We will test one complete example from society approval to resident booking and final Report Card.

At the end of Week 1, PetSaathi will have a complete, consistent and outreach-ready society partnership kit.”

A monthly compliance watch for DPDP, WhatsApp, AWBI and consumer-rule changes would keep these materials current as Phase 7 expands.

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Week 2: Build the Society Target List 🏢🐾

Week 2 objective

Create a verified, prioritised and outreach-ready list of 50 apartment societies in one city—not a random spreadsheet of society names.

By Day 14, every priority society should have:

Society profile

+ demand estimate

+ operational fit

+ contact source

+ decision-maker path

+ priority score

+ personalised message

+ next action

The final outcome should be:

50 researched societies, 15–20 high-priority targets, 10–15 personalised first contacts and a clean CRM for follow-up.

1. Correct structure for the 50-society list

Do not treat all 50 societies equally.

Recommended segmentation

### Table 57

| Segment | Count | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Tier A — Priority launch targets | 15 | Contact during Week 2–3 |
| Tier B — Qualified prospects | 20 | Contact after testing first messages |
| Tier C — Nurture prospects | 10 | Require more research or introductions |
| Tier D — Strategic experiments | 5 | Large township, premium project or unusual opportunity |

Geographic rule

The 50 societies should come from only two or three nearby micro-markets.

For an Ahmedabad pilot, an example could be:

Cluster 1: Bopal

Cluster 2: South Bopal

Cluster 3: Satellite / Prahlad Nagar

Avoid building a list spread across the entire city. Society density is valuable because the same sitter pool, emergency contacts and operations team can support several nearby communities.

2. Improved Society CRM columns

Your proposed columns are a good beginning, but the CRM needs separate fields for research, qualification, contacts, operations and next actions.

A. Society identity

### Table 58

| Column | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Society ID | SOC-AHD-001 |
| Society name | Green Acres |
| City | Ahmedabad |
| Area | Bopal |
| Complete locality | South Bopal Extension |
| Address | Publicly listed address |
| Map reference | Google Maps listing |
| Society type | Premium gated apartment |
| Project status | Occupied / partly occupied / unknown |
| Number of towers | 8 |
| Estimated flats | 500 |
| Estimated occupied flats | 425 |
| Research confidence | High / Medium / Low |

Important

Do not enter 500 flats merely because someone guessed it.

Store:

Flats estimate

Source

Date checked

Confidence

Possible sources include the society’s official website, public property information, RERA records, mapping data or confirmation from management.

For Ahmedabad, the official GujRERA portal can help identify registered residential projects and promoter information. However, a RERA entry is a discovery and verification source—it does not by itself prove current occupancy, active management or pet-parent demand.

B. Pet-demand information

### Table 59

| Column | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Estimated pet families — low | 25 |
| Estimated pet families — likely | 45 |
| Estimated pet families — high | 60 |
| Estimate confidence | Medium |
| Evidence source | Vet referral + resident group |
| Active pet-parent group | Yes |
| Existing dog walkers visible | Yes |
| Pet events previously held | Unknown |
| Most likely service | Daily dog walking |
| Secondary service | Pet sitting |
| Peak expected time | 6:30–9:00 AM |

Do not use one unexplained exact number such as:

Estimated pet families: 50

Use a range:

Low estimate: 30

Likely estimate: 45

High estimate: 60

Confidence: Medium

C. Decision-maker contacts

### Table 60

| Column | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Resident champion | Priya S. |
| RWA/committee contact | Secretary |
| Facility manager | Rahul M. |
| Property-management company | ABC Facility Services |
| Security supervisor | Collected after interest |
| Public phone | Business contact |
| Public email | manager@... |
| LinkedIn profile | Public professional profile |
| Contact source | Society website |
| Permission status | Public B2B contact |
| Preferred channel | Email / call |
| Last verified | 18 July 2026 |

Privacy rule

Names, phone numbers and email addresses are personal data. Record:

Where the information came from

Why PetSaathi needs it

Who can access it

Whether the person asked not to be contacted

When it should be deleted

India’s Digital Personal Data Protection Rules, 2025 were published on November 14, 2025, with staged commencement provisions. The CRM should therefore be built with purpose limitation, controlled access and retention rules from the beginning.

D. Operational fit

### Table 61

| Column | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Nearby active walkers | 4 |
| Nearby backup walkers | 2 |
| Nearby pet sitters | 3 |
| Boarding partner nearby | 1 |
| Veterinary partner nearby | 2 |
| Distance from launch hub | 2.4 km |
| Estimated travel time | 12 minutes |
| Gate complexity | Medium |
| Sitter registration needed | Yes |
| Resident event possible | Yes |
| Morning capacity | Strong |
| Evening capacity | Limited |
| Operational fit score | 82/100 |

Google Places can help discover and verify publicly listed societies, clinics, pet shops and other points of interest, but important operational data must still be confirmed directly. The Places API provides structured location information about establishments and geographic places.

3. Recommended CRM stages

Replace the broad status:

Contacted / Interested / Signed

with a controlled pipeline:

IDENTIFIED

QUALIFIED

INTRODUCTION_REQUESTED

CONTACTED

REPLIED

DISCOVERY_SCHEDULED

DISCOVERY_COMPLETED

RESIDENT_SURVEY

PROPOSAL_SENT

COMMITTEE_REVIEW

CONVERTED

Owner

Current stage

A society without a next action is not being actively managed.

4. Sources for building the list

Use a layered research process.

Source 1 — Existing PetSaathi contacts

Start with:

Existing pet parents

Internship or college network

Friends and family

Local pet owners

Previous enquiries

Existing sitter contacts

Ask:

“Which apartment societies in Bopal or South Bopal have active pet-parent communities?”

Warm introductions generally provide better access to committee members than cold outreach.

Source 2 — Official and public property data

Use:

State RERA portal

Society or developer website

Public property-management information

Publicly listed project documents

Mapping platforms

For Gujarat, GujRERA is the official project and agent registration portal.

Use RERA information to identify:

Project name

Developer

Area

Approximate project scale

Registration details

Do not treat it as proof that:

All homes are occupied

An RWA is active

The listed promoter manages the property

The society contains many pet families

Source 3 — Google Maps and locality research

Search terms:

premium apartment society Bopal

gated community South Bopal

residential township Satellite Ahmedabad

apartment society near pet clinic Bopal

Record:

Correct spelling

Area

Public website

Public phone

Approximate scale

Nearby parks

Nearby veterinary clinics

Nearby pet shops

Do not copy all map listings blindly. Confirm that the location is residential and currently relevant.

Source 4 — Veterinarians and pet shops

Ask partners:

Which societies send many customers?

Which localities have daily walking demand?

Which societies organise pet events?

Which societies have active pet-parent groups?

Which managers are open to pet initiatives?

Correct referral method

Ask the partner to:

Introduce PetSaathi

Forward a QR code

Share the pilot information

Invite PetSaathi to a joint meeting

Do not ask the clinic or shop to hand over its customer database.

Source 5 — Dog parks and walking locations

Observe:

Which society gates dog owners come from

Busy walking periods

Existing independent walkers

Common complaints

Nearby clusters

Use voluntary conversations. Do not secretly photograph residents or collect contact information without permission.

Source 6 — LinkedIn

Search for:

Facility Manager + Bopal

Community Manager + Ahmedabad

Property Manager + apartment

RWA Secretary + society name

LinkedIn limits invitations to prevent misuse and may restrict accounts that send too many or use prohibited automation. It recommends sending relevant and thoughtful invitations rather than high-volume requests.

Source 7 — Resident groups

Use:

Society Facebook groups

Pet-parent groups

Local community pages

Resident referrals

WhatsApp introductions

Do not enter or scrape closed community groups using false identities.

5. How to identify a “pet-heavy” society

Do not rely on one signal.

Strong signals

Active resident pet-parent group

Previous vaccination or grooming camp

Multiple pet-related businesses nearby

Several visible dog walkers during peak hours

Resident champion confirms demand

Facility manager reports recurring pet-care requests

Society has a pet-related notice or process

Existing PetSaathi lead resides there

Medium signals

Large occupied premium project

Suitable walking environment

Nearby veterinary clinic

Multiple towers and family households

Society organises resident events

Security already manages external service providers

Weak signals

Expensive property

Many flats

Attractive website

Good online reviews

A premium society is not automatically pet-heavy.

6. Society priority scoring model

Use a 100-point score.

### Table 64

| Factor | Weight |
| --- | --- |
| Verified or likely resident demand | 25 |
| Local sitter and backup capacity | 20 |
| Geographic cluster fit | 15 |
| Access to decision-maker | 15 |
| Security and operational feasibility | 10 |
| Society size and occupancy | 10 |
| Vet/pet-shop partner ecosystem | 5 |

Score interpretation

### Table 65

| Score | Segment | Action |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 80–100 | Tier A | Contact immediately |
| 65–79 | Tier B | Qualified outreach |
| 50–64 | Tier C | Research/nurture |
| Below 50 | Hold | Do not spend active sales time |

Automatic hold conditions

Even a high score should be paused when:

No local sitter supply exists

Travel time is unreasonable

Society requests unlawful pet-control activity

No authorised contact can be identified

The project is not occupied

Contact data came from a questionable source

The society is outside the launch cluster

Day 8 — List the top 20 premium societies

Goal

Create an initial longlist of 20 societies with enough scale, demand potential and geographic fit.

Tasks

1. Choose the launch clusters

Example:

Bopal

South Bopal

Satellite

2. Identify 30–40 candidates

Use:

GujRERA

Google Maps

Public developer websites

Existing resident contacts

Vet/pet-shop referrals

3. Reduce to the best 20

Remove:

Unoccupied projects

Commercial developments

Properties outside the service radius

Societies without local sitter coverage

Duplicate project names

Projects with unclear identity

4. Assign initial confidence

High confidence:

Multiple reliable sources

Medium:

One reliable source plus supporting evidence

Low:

Unconfirmed directory or word-of-mouth entry

Day 8 output

20 premium or strategically valuable societies

with identity, location, scale and source fields completed

Day 9 — Collect manager and RWA contacts

Goal

Find the right contact path—not merely any resident’s phone number.

Contact priority

1. Warm resident introduction

2. Facility manager

3. RWA/AOA secretary or president

4. Property-management company

5. Public society email

6. LinkedIn professional contact

Security staff should not be treated as the commercial decision-maker.

Contact research checklist

For each society, try to identify:

Resident champion

Operational contact

Authorised decision-maker

Example:

Resident champion: Pet-parent group admin

Operational contact: Facility manager

Decision-maker: RWA secretary

Data-quality rules

Store:

Contact name

Role

Source

Date verified

Public or referred

Preferred communication method

Do-not-contact status

Day 9 output

20 societies with at least one contact path

10 societies with two contact paths

5 societies with all three stakeholders identified

Day 10 — Identify pet-heavy societies

Goal

Separate genuinely promising societies from projects that only appear premium.

Research actions

Speak with nearby vets

Speak with pet shops

Review public society posts

Ask resident champions

Visit selected local walking areas

Check for prior camps or pet events

Check existing PetSaathi enquiries

Observe peak walking activity respectfully

Create demand ratings

CONFIRMED:

Resident or manager confirms meaningful demand

LIKELY:

Several supporting signals

POSSIBLE:

Large society but little direct evidence

UNKNOWN:

No useful demand evidence

Day 10 output

At least 10 confirmed/likely pet-heavy societies

and demand-confidence labels for all 20 premium targets

Day 11 — Contact veterinarians and pet shops for referrals

Goal

Use trusted local businesses to discover additional societies and obtain warm introductions.

Partner list

Build:

5–10 veterinary clinics

5–10 pet shops

3–5 groomers or trainers

Questions

Ask:

Which societies have the largest pet-parent customer base?

Which societies request vaccination or grooming camps?

Which areas have recurring dog-walking demand?

Can the partner introduce PetSaathi to a resident or manager?

Would the partner participate in a society event?

Referral recording

### Table 66

| Field | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Partner | ABC Veterinary Clinic |
| Society referred | Green Acres |
| Introduction status | Requested |
| Partner interest | Vaccination camp |
| Commercial expectation | Fixed event fee |
| Next action | Send one-page summary |

Day 11 output

10+ partner conversations

10–20 additional society names

3–5 warm introduction opportunities

Updated 50-society longlist

Day 12 — Segment societies by priority

Goal

Convert the list into a sales plan.

Steps

1. Remove invalid records

Remove or hold:

Duplicate societies

Unoccupied projects

Far-away communities

No sitter coverage

No reliable contact source

No likely resident demand

2. Score all 50 societies

Apply the 100-point model.

3. Assign tiers

Tier A: 15 societies

Tier B: 20 societies

Tier C: 10 societies

Strategic: 5 societies

4. Assign a package hypothesis

Examples:

Green Acres:

No-Fee Pilot → Basic Partner

Premium Township:

No-Fee Pilot → Premium Pet-Care Desk

Low-demand society:

Event/Camp Model first

5. Assign an owner

Each Tier A target must have one PetSaathi team owner.

Day 12 output

Final 50-society CRM

with segments, scores, owners and recommended first offers

Day 13 — Prepare personalised messages

Goal

Create one relevant message for every Tier A society.

Do not use exactly the same message 15 times.

Personalisation fields

Each message should reference one or two genuine points:

Society name

Area

Existing resident introduction

Society size

Likely service need

Nearby sitter availability

Local partner referral

Relevant event interest

Message structure

1. Who you are

2. Why you selected this society

3. The relevant society problem

4. PetSaathi’s solution

5. Pilot offer

6. One clear CTA

Example logic

Society with daily walking interest

“Several residents in Bopal have asked about reliable morning dog-walking support…”

Society referred by veterinarian

“ABC Veterinary Clinic suggested that your community has an active pet-parent group…”

Premium township

“Your multi-tower access structure makes an approved sitter roster and central pet-care desk especially useful…”

Do not mention pet-family numbers unless they have been reliably sourced.

Channel preparation

For every Tier A society, prepare:

Email version

WhatsApp version

LinkedIn version

Phone-call opener

Relevant attachment

Preferred send time

Follow-up dates

For WhatsApp Business outreach, Meta requires user opt-in before message templates are sent; opt-in should identify the business and messaging purpose. Messages should be personalised and useful.

Day 13 output

15 personalised outreach packs

Each pack contains:

Primary message

Secondary channel

Contact name

Personalisation reason

CTA

Follow-up schedule

Goal

Test outreach quality before contacting all 50 societies.

Recommended batch size

Contact 10–15 societies, not all 50.

Recommended composition

### Table 67

9. Common Week 2 mistakes

Mistake 1 — Filling the CRM with unverified estimates

Correction: Store source, date and confidence.

Mistake 2 — Selecting societies only because they are premium

Correction: Demand and local sitter supply matter more than property price.

Mistake 3 — Building a city-wide list

Correction: Concentrate on two or three nearby clusters.

Mistake 4 — Treating every contact as a decision-maker

Correction: Separate resident champion, operational contact and authorised approver.

Mistake 5 — Buying or scraping private contact lists

Correction: Use public business contacts, professional profiles, warm introductions and opt-in forms.

Mistake 6 — Asking clinics for customer data

Correction: Request an introduction or ask the clinic to share PetSaathi’s QR link.

Mistake 7 — Sending the same message to all 50 societies

Correction: Personalise the reason for approaching each Tier A society.

Mistake 8 — Contacting all 50 on the first day

Correction: Test 10–15 contacts, analyse results and improve the message.

Mistake 9 — No next-action date

Correction: Every active CRM record must have an owner and next follow-up.

Week 2 definition of done

Week 2 is complete only when:

Research

50 valid societies are recorded.

Duplicates and unoccupied projects are removed.

Every record has a research source.

Estimates include confidence levels.

Demand

At least 15 societies have confirmed or likely pet demand.

Pet-heavy status is supported by evidence.

Local service needs are identified.

Contacts

At least 35 societies have a valid contact path.

At least 20 have a decision-maker or operational contact.

Contact sources and permissions are recorded.

Do-not-contact requests can be enforced.

Operations

Tier A societies have local sitter capacity.

Travel and backup feasibility are checked.

No priority society depends on one unavailable sitter.

Outreach

Fifteen personalised message packs are ready.

Ten to fifteen societies receive Batch 1 outreach.

Every contact is logged.

Every active opportunity has a follow-up date.

Simple explanation for professor

“During Week 2, PetSaathi will create a researched list of 50 apartment societies in one city.

We will not select societies only because they are large or premium. We will check resident pet demand, nearby sitter availability, society access, decision-maker contacts and geographic distance.

On Day 8, we will identify the first 20 premium or strategically useful societies. On Day 9, we will find facility managers, RWA members and resident contacts. On Day 10, we will identify which societies have genuine pet-parent demand.

On Day 11, we will speak with local veterinary clinics, pet shops, groomers and trainers for society referrals. We will request introductions instead of asking them to share private customer lists.

On Day 12, all 50 societies will be scored and divided into priority groups. On Day 13, personalised WhatsApp, email and LinkedIn messages will be prepared for the highest-priority societies.

On Day 14, PetSaathi will contact only the first 10 to 15 societies. We will measure replies, meeting requests and objections before contacting the remaining list.

The final CRM will show the society name, location, estimated size, pet demand, contacts, operational fit, priority score, current sales stage and next follow-up date. This creates a focused sales pipeline instead of an unorganised contact list.”

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Week 3: Society Outreach and Meetings 🏢🐾

Week 3 objective

Convert the Week 2 target list into real partnership discussions.

By the end of Week 3, PetSaathi should achieve:

### Table 70

| Result | Target |
| --- | --- |
| Qualified societies contacted | 30 |
| Positive responses | 8–12 |
| Meetings scheduled or completed | 5–8 |
| Resident-demand surveys approved | 3–5 |
| Pilot proposals requested | 2–4 |
| CRM records with a next action | 100% |

The main objective is not to send 30 messages. It is to create 5–8 serious conversations with authorised or influential society stakeholders.

1. Define what “contacted” means

A society should count as contacted only when:

✓ A relevant contact has been identified

✓ The contact source has been recorded

✓ The message is personalised

✓ The message has been delivered through an appropriate channel

✓ The activity is recorded in the CRM

✓ A follow-up date is assigned

Do not count:

Generic messages sent to an unverified number

Instagram or Facebook comments

Messages sent only to security guards

Unanswered calls without a follow-up message

Bulk WhatsApp broadcasts to unknown contacts

LinkedIn connection requests without context

Required contact roles

Try to identify at least two of these for every Tier A society:

### Table 71

| Role | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Resident champion | Proves pet-parent demand |
| Facility manager | Explains daily operations |
| RWA/AOA representative | Approves partnership |
| Property manager | Coordinates large communities |
| Pet-parent group administrator | Enables resident survey |
| Security supervisor | Designs access after approval |

Security should normally be involved after the committee or facility manager expresses interest.

2. Recommended Week 3 operating flow

Review priority CRM

↓

Research contact before outreach

↓

Send personalised introduction

↓

Follow up through a second channel

↓

Schedule discovery meeting

↓

Conduct 30-minute meeting

↓

Send meeting summary

↓

Launch resident-demand survey

↓

Prepare pilot proposal

Each step should have a clear CRM stage, owner and deadline.

Your proposed schedule is good. The following version adds preparation, meeting documentation and proposal work.

| Time | Task | Expected output |

| 18:00–18:15 | Daily review | Tomorrow’s priority list |

Important operating rule

Do not postpone CRM updates until the end of the week.

Update the CRM immediately after:

Calls

Replies

Meetings

Objections

Introductions

Follow-up promises

Proposal requests

This prevents lost context and duplicate outreach.

4. Suggested Day 15–21 execution plan

Day 15 — Prepare the outreach batch

Goal

Select the first 10–12 societies to contact.

Recommended mix

### Table 73

| Lead type | Count |
| --- | --- |
| Warm resident introductions | 3–4 |
| Vet or pet-shop referrals | 2–3 |
| Facility-manager contacts | 3–4 |
| Cold strategic targets | 1–2 |

Tasks

Verify contact names and roles.

Review society size and likely demand.

Check nearby sitter capacity.

Identify the best starting service.

Prepare a personalised message.

Set follow-up dates before sending.

Output

10–12 complete outreach packs

Day 16 — First outreach batch

Goal

Contact the first batch using the strongest available channel.

Channel order

Warm introduction

→ Email or call

→ Permission-based WhatsApp

→ LinkedIn

First-contact objective

Request a 15-minute introductory call.

Do not ask the society to approve the full partnership in the first message.

Output

10–12 initial contacts

Delivery and response status

Follow-up tasks scheduled

Message objections recorded

Day 17 — Follow-ups and first meetings

Goal

Convert responses into discovery calls.

Follow-up message structure

Reference earlier message

↓

Restate one relevant benefit

↓

Offer two specific meeting times

↓

Keep message under 100 words

Example:

“Hi Mr. Shah, following up on the PetSaathi society pilot shared on Monday. The programme may be particularly useful for organising approved dog walkers and recurring morning slots in Green Acres. Would Wednesday at 3:00 PM or Thursday at 11:30 AM work for a 15-minute discussion?”

First meetings conducted

Day 18 — Second outreach batch

Goal

Improve:

Call-to-action

Attachment length

Channel selection
