A paid society plan

Profitable events

But the full ₹63,000 resident GMV is not PetSaathi’s margin.

6. Is ₹25,000–₹1 lakh monthly resident GMV realistic?

₹25,000 monthly GMV

This is realistic with a mixed model.

Examples:

100 walks × ₹200 = ₹20,000

10 sitting visits × ₹500 = ₹5,000

Total = ₹25,000

Or:

50 service units × average ₹500

= ₹25,000

₹1 lakh monthly GMV

At 150 services, ₹1 lakh requires:

₹1,00,000 ÷ 150

= approximately ₹667 average GMV per service

That is high for a walking-heavy society.

At ₹250 per walk, PetSaathi would need:

400 dog-walk service units

to generate ₹1 lakh in walking GMV.

Therefore, ₹1 lakh with 150 services generally requires:

Multi-night boarding orders

Premium sitting

Package purchases

Event revenue

Larger service durations

Multiple-pet bookings

Corrected recommendation

Use:

### Table 119

| Society stage | Likely monthly resident GMV |
| --- | --- |
| Early pilot | ₹10,000–₹25,000 |
| Stable Basic society | ₹25,000–₹50,000 |
| Strong mixed-service society | ₹50,000–₹75,000 |
| Premium/high-density upside | ₹75,000–₹1 lakh+ |

The upper range should be treated as an upside target, not a default assumption.

7. Correct the “total potential revenue” table

Your original table adds:

Resident bookings

+ society subscription

+ camp revenue

This combines three different accounting categories.

Correct structure

### Table 120

| Revenue layer | Correct measurement |
| --- | --- |
| Resident care bookings | GMV |
| PetSaathi care margin | Platform service margin/contribution |
| Society subscription | PetSaathi subscription revenue |
| Events | PetSaathi coordination revenue or net event contribution |
| Partner payouts | Cost/pass-through |
| Sitter payouts | Cost/pass-through |

Better monthly model

### Table 121

| Source | Low | Strong |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Resident service GMV | ₹25,000 | ₹1,00,000 |
| PetSaathi service margin at 30% | ₹7,500 | ₹30,000 |
| Society subscription | ₹5,000 | ₹25,000 |
| Event contribution | ₹5,000 | ₹20,000 |
| Gross PetSaathi contribution before support | ₹17,500 | ₹75,000 |

This is more accurate than calling ₹35,000–₹1.75 lakh all “revenue.”

Whether PetSaathi legally reports gross resident GMV or only its net commission as accounting revenue depends on whether it acts as principal or marketplace agent. Confirm the revenue-recognition structure with a chartered accountant.

8. Society-specific cost allocation

A society can appear profitable until its hidden operating costs are included.

Track:

### Table 122

| Cost | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Coordinator allocation | Time spent on society support |
| Customer support | Calls, WhatsApp and complaints |
| Sitter recruitment | Verification and training |
| Society onboarding | Meetings, access and setup |
| Resident discount | ₹99 launch subsidy |
| Payment gateway | Transaction cost |
| Replacement cost | Backup sitter incentives |
| Incident reserve | Refunds, recovery and support |
| Event staffing | Coordinator and travel |
| Technology | Notifications, media and maps |

True society contribution

True society contribution =

Service contribution

+ subscription

+ event contribution

− all society-specific variable costs

Do not scale a society that produces good GMV but negative true contribution.

9. Society conversion funnel

Your funnel is correct, but add qualification and activation stages:

Societies listed

↓

Societies qualified

↓

Decision-makers contacted

↓

Meetings completed

↓

Resident survey approved

↓

Pilot signed

↓

Pilot activated

↓

Resident announcement sent

↓

Qualified pet-parent leads

↓

First paying households

↓

Completed services

↓

Repeat completed services

↓

Paid society partnership

The difference between pilot signed and pilot activated is important. A signed society with no landing page, sitter capacity or resident campaign is not operational.

10. Funnel target analysis

Contacted → meeting: 20–30%

If PetSaathi contacts 30 qualified societies:

30 contacts

→ 6–9 meetings

If it contacts 50:

50 contacts

→ 10–15 meetings

This target is reasonable for carefully selected and personalised outreach.

Meeting → pilot: 30–50%

From 6–9 meetings:

Approximately 2–5 pilots

From 10–15 meetings:

Approximately 3–8 pilots

The upper range will normally require warm introductions, confirmed resident demand and local sitter availability.

Pilot → resident leads: 30+ leads

This should mean:

30 qualified, opted-in resident leads per activated society.

Do not count:

Society WhatsApp members

Poster views

Anonymous website traffic

Residents who never expressed pet-care interest

Leads → first paying households: 20–40%

For 30 qualified leads:

6–12 first paying households

Track first paying households, not simply bookings. One customer may create several bookings and distort acquisition conversion.

First paying households → repeat: 25–40%

For 6–12 first customers:

Approximately 2–5 repeat customers

The strongest repeat metric is:

The customer completed a second paid service.

Not merely:

Clicked “book again”

Asked about prices

Viewed a package

Said they were interested

Pilot → paid society plan: 20–30%

This is a reasonable early-stage target, but it produces slow B2B conversion.

If PetSaathi launches 3–8 pilots:

Expected paid partnerships:

approximately 1–2

To secure five paid society partners at a 25% pilot conversion rate, PetSaathi would need roughly:

20 pilots

If 40% of meetings become pilots:

50 completed meetings

If 25% of contacted societies become meetings:

200 qualified society contacts

This shows why the paid-society model needs a long and repeatable acquisition pipeline.

11. Correct funnel KPIs

### Table 123

| Funnel stage | Target |
| --- | --- |
| Qualified society → contact | 80%+ |
| Contact → response | 20–40% |
| Response → meeting | 40–60% |
| Contact → meeting | 20–30% |
| Meeting → resident survey | 40–60% |
| Meeting → pilot | 30–50% |
| Signed pilot → activated pilot | 80%+ |
| Lead → first paying household | 20–40% |
| First paying household → second completed service | 25–40% |
| Pilot → paid society plan | 20–30% initially |
| Paid society → renewal | 70%+ target after proven fit |

The renewal rate becomes more important than the initial pilot conversion once the model matures.

12. Paid-partnership readiness criteria

A society should not convert to paid merely because the free period has ended.

Minimum commercial criteria

### Table 124

| Metric | Minimum |
| --- | --- |
| Qualified pet-parent leads | 30+ |
| Unique paying households | 8–15 |
| Paid booking orders | 15+ |
| Completed services | 90%+ of due bookings |
| Repeat customers | 5+ |
| Average rating | 4.5+ |
| Rating sample | Preferably 10+ reviews |
| Report Cards delivered | 98%+ |
| Security-entry success | 97%+ |
| Critical unresolved incidents | 0 |
| RWA/facility feedback | Positive |
| Society contribution | Positive or clearly improving |

Additional recommendation

Do not use only total booking count.

For example:

15 bookings from 2 households

is weaker than:

15 bookings from 10 households

The second case proves broader resident value.

13. Recommended paid conversion offer

For a society with:

42 resident registrations

28 paid bookings

4.7 average rating

12 repeat requests

first confirm:

How many unique paying households?

How many services were completed?

How many repeat services were actually paid?

What was the society’s monthly GMV?

What was PetSaathi’s contribution?

How much support did the society require?

If the numbers are healthy, the offer can be:

Basic Society Partner — ₹9,999/month plus applicable taxes

Include:

Society landing page

Resident partner benefits

Priority eligible-sitter matching

Approved-access sitter roster

Dedicated WhatsApp routing

Monthly aggregate report

Named operations owner

Incident and complaint escalation

Monthly programme review

The ₹9,999 recommendation is appropriate only when expected subscription and service contribution can cover the society’s support and coordination workload.

15. Final corrected society economics

For a 500-flat society, the following is a more defensible target:

40–70 possible pet households

↓

20–30 qualified leads

↓

10–20 paying households

↓

50–150 monthly service units

↓

₹12,000–₹63,000 typical resident GMV

↓

₹3,000–₹19,000 service contribution

↓

+ ₹5,000–₹25,000 society retainer

↓

+ ₹0–₹15,000 event contribution

↓

− coordinator, support and acquisition costs

↓

Net society contribution

₹1 lakh+ resident GMV remains possible, but generally requires:

More than 150 low-value walks

Multi-night boarding

Premium sitting

Package purchases

Multiple pets

Paid events

A larger number of active households

Final operating principle

Do not scale societies based on bookings or GMV alone. Scale the societies that produce repeat customers, reliable service delivery and positive contribution after sitter payouts, discounts, payment fees and support costs.

Simple explanation for professor

“A 500-flat society may have approximately 40 to 70 pet-owning families, but this is only an initial estimate. PetSaathi must confirm the actual number using resident surveys and registrations.

Out of these families, around 20 to 30 may initially show interest, and 10 to 20 may become paying customers. These customers may generate 50 to 150 monthly service units such as dog walks, sitting visits or boarding nights.

The total money paid by residents is called GMV. It is not all PetSaathi revenue because a major portion must be paid to walkers, sitters and boarding partners. PetSaathi’s actual contribution is calculated after sitter payouts, payment-gateway fees, discounts, refunds and support costs.

The society can also pay a monthly subscription, and PetSaathi can earn coordination revenue from events. These amounts should be added to PetSaathi’s service contribution—not to the full resident GMV.

The society funnel begins with identifying societies, contacting decision-makers, conducting meetings, launching pilots, collecting resident leads, generating paid bookings and converting repeat demand into a paid society partnership.

A society should move to a paid package only when it has enough leads, paying households, repeat customers, strong ratings, successful security access and positive economics.”

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Society Dashboard and Partnership Agreement 🏢🐾

Executive decision

Approve both components, with one clear separation:

The society dashboard measures demand, service quality, supply health, safety and commercial value.

The partnership agreement defines what PetSaathi and the society may—and may not—do.

The operating principle should be:

PetSaathi provides pet-care services and coordinates caregiver access. It is not a pet-rule enforcement agency, animal-control authority or substitute for municipal and veterinary authorities.

AWBI maintains specific guidance for pet owners, caregivers, RWAs and apartment-owner associations. Its 2015 guidance states that RWAs and AOAs cannot introduce blanket bans on keeping pet dogs. The partnership should therefore support responsible coexistence and organised services, not pet prohibition or removal.

Part A — Society Dashboard

1. Dashboard purpose

The dashboard should answer seven questions:

Is there real resident demand?

Are residents actually booking and paying?

Are services being completed reliably?

Are customers returning?

Is the sitter pool healthy?

Are complaints and incidents controlled?

Is the society commercially valuable?

The dashboard should not be a decorative analytics page. Every metric should lead to a decision.

2. Dashboard access levels

Do not show the same dashboard to everyone.

PetSaathi admin dashboard

PetSaathi operations may view:

Resident registrations

Individual bookings

Payment status

Sitter assignments

Pet-care controls

Complaints and incidents

Refunds

Revenue and payout information

Society management dashboard

The RWA, facility manager or authorised society representative should generally see only aggregate programme information:

Total resident leads

Active customers

Completed services

On-time rate

Aggregate complaint count

Security-entry issues

Event participation

Monthly society performance

They should not automatically receive:

Individual Pet Profiles

Medical information

Bite history

Personal emergency contacts

Full resident phone numbers

Private Report Cards

GPS routes

Individual complaint narratives

Payment credentials

The final DPDP Rules were published on November 14, 2025 with staged commencement provisions. PetSaathi should therefore use purpose-limited access, clear notices, reasonable security controls and a published contact for data-related questions or grievances.

3. Core dashboard metrics

A. Society identity

### Table 125

| Metric | Example | Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Society name | Green Acres | Account identification |
| City and area | Ahmedabad, Bopal | Geographic reporting |
| Society status | Active pilot | Partnership lifecycle |
| Package | Basic Partner | Commercial tracking |
| Pilot start/end | 1–30 August | Contract control |
| Operations owner | Prince | Internal accountability |
| Society contact | Facility manager | Operational escalation |

Recommended society statuses:

PROSPECT

PILOT_APPROVED

SETUP_IN_PROGRESS

ACTIVE_PILOT

PAID_PARTNER

PAUSED

ENDED

B. Resident demand

Resident leads

A valid lead is a resident who:

Registered voluntarily

Provided a usable contact

Identified a pet-care need

Accepted the relevant privacy notice

Belongs to the society

Do not count poster impressions or all WhatsApp-group members as leads.

Dashboard fields

### Table 126

| Metric | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Resident leads | 42 |
| Verified residents | 35 |
| Pet Profiles started | 31 |
| Pet Profiles completed | 26 |
| Lead-to-profile conversion | 61.9% |
| Trial requests | 22 |

Formula

Lead-to-profile conversion =

Completed Pet Profiles ÷ Resident leads × 100

This tells you whether residents are genuinely interested enough to complete onboarding.

C. Active customers

An active customer should normally mean a resident who has completed at least one paid service within the selected reporting period.

Track separately:

REGISTERED_RESIDENT

PAID_CUSTOMER

ACTIVE_CUSTOMER

REPEAT_CUSTOMER

INACTIVE_CUSTOMER

### Table 127

| Metric | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Paid customers | 18 |
| Active this month | 14 |
| First-time customers | 8 |
| Repeat customers | 6 |
| Reactivated customers | 2 |

Do not classify someone as an active customer only because they joined a society group.

D. Bookings and usage

### Table 128

| Metric | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Booking requests | Demand before payment |
| Paid bookings | Real commercial conversion |
| Confirmed bookings | Operational commitments |
| Completed bookings | Successfully delivered services |
| Cancelled bookings | Service loss |
| Refunded bookings | Financial recovery |
| Bookings per active resident | Usage depth |
| Bookings per society per week | Marketplace density |

Important formulas

Booking conversion =

Paid bookings ÷ Qualified resident leads × 100

Completion rate =

Completed due bookings ÷ Total due bookings × 100

Booking density =

Completed bookings ÷ Active society weeks

Keep these booking states separate:

REQUESTED

PAID

CONFIRMED

COMPLETED

CANCELLED

REFUNDED

Do not report 50 booking requests as 50 completed bookings.

E. Repeat customers and retention

Repeat demand is the strongest proof that the society partnership is useful.

Track:

### Table 129

| Metric | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Repeat interest | Customer asks about booking again |
| Repeat request | Second booking request created |
| Second paid booking | Customer pays again |
| Second completed service | Strongest early retention signal |
| Package purchased | Resident buys walking plan |
| Package utilisation | Resident actually uses credits |
| Same-sitter request | Trust and continuity signal |

Recommended repeat-rate formula

Repeat customer rate =

Customers with a second completed service

÷

Customers whose repeat window has matured

× 100

Do not divide by customers who completed their first booking yesterday and have not had enough time to return.

F. Top service

Track bookings and contribution by service:

### Table 130

| Service | Paid bookings | Completed | Repeat rate | Rating |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Dog walking | 72 | 69 | 38% | 4.7 |
| Pet sitting | 18 | 17 | 24% | 4.6 |
| Boarding beta | 3 | 3 | — | 4.5 |
| Grooming camp | 21 appointments | 20 | — | 4.4 |

The top service should not be chosen using booking volume alone.

Evaluate:

Demand

+ completion rate

+ repeat rate

+ contribution

+ complaint rate

+ operational difficulty

Dog walking may have the highest volume, while sitting may generate higher contribution per booking.

G. Sitter-pool health

The dashboard should measure service capacity—not merely sitter headcount.

### Table 131

| Metric | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| Active walkers | Available supply |
| Active pet sitters | Sitting capacity |
| Backup candidates | Recovery capability |
| Morning slot capacity | Peak demand control |
| Evening slot capacity | Peak demand control |
| Average sitter utilisation | Workload balance |
| Single-sitter dependency | Continuity risk |
| Verification expiry | Compliance risk |
| Training expiry | Capability risk |
| Low reliability sitters | Quality warning |

Useful alerts

NO_BACKUP_AVAILABLE

PEAK_SLOT_ABOVE_85_PERCENT

ONE_SITTER_HANDLES_ABOVE_50_PERCENT

VERIFICATION_EXPIRING

TRAINING_EXPIRED

HIGH_CANCELLATION_RATE

A society with five registered walkers can still have weak supply if only one is available during the required morning period.

H. Complaints and incidents

Do not combine ordinary complaints and safety incidents.

Complaint examples

Delayed response

Billing question

Sitter communication problem

Resident dissatisfaction

Entry confusion

Incident examples

Pet injury

Bite

Escape

Unsafe handling

Medical emergency

Serious property issue

Sitter no-show during a critical service

Track:

### Table 132

| Metric | Target |
| --- | --- |
| Complaints per 100 completed bookings | Below 5 |
| Critical unresolved incidents | 0 |
| Active-service response time | Under 5–10 minutes |
| Complaints acknowledged same day | 100% |
| Incidents with assigned owner | 100% |
| Corrective actions completed | 100% by due date |

Complaint-rate formula

Complaint rate =

Formal complaints ÷ Completed bookings × 100

A society with two complaints from ten services has a more serious pattern than one with two complaints from two hundred services.

I. Monthly financial value

Do not show one ambiguous figure called “revenue.”

Track separately:

### Table 133

| Metric | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Resident booking GMV | Total value paid by residents |
| Society subscription fee | Monthly society package charge |
| Event GMV | Total event service value |
| Sitter/partner payouts | Amount owed to providers |
| Refunds | Amount returned |
| Payment and support costs | Direct service costs |
| Platform service contribution | Value retained before broader overhead |
| Net society contribution | Value after direct society operating costs |

Recommended model

Monthly society contribution =

Society subscription fee

+ platform contribution from resident bookings

+ event coordination contribution

− resident discounts funded by PetSaathi

− direct coordinator/support allocation

− society-specific marketing cost

− refunds and recovery cost

Accounting recognition and GST treatment should be confirmed with a chartered accountant; the operating dashboard should still keep each component separate.

J. Event participation

Track each event through a funnel:

Residents reached

↓

Registered

↓

Attended

↓

Service completed

↓

Pet Profile created

↓

Trial booked

↓

Repeat customer

### Table 134

| Metric | Example |
| --- | --- |
| Event registrations | 40 |
| Attendance | 30 |
| Attendance rate | 75% |
| Qualified leads | 22 |
| Pet Profiles completed | 12 |
| Trial bookings | 7 |
| Event rating | 4.6 |
| Event contribution | ₹8,500 |

A successful event is not merely one with many photographs or attendees. It should create trust, leads, bookings or commercial value.

4. Recommended dashboard sections

Show:

5. Dashboard health score

Use a simple Red–Amber–Green status.

Green

15+ monthly paid bookings

90%+ completion

4.5+ rating

Repeat customers present

No critical unresolved incident

Local backup coverage

Positive or improving contribution

Amber

Demand exists but repeat rate is weak

Rating between 4.0 and 4.49

Entry failures are increasing

Sitter capacity is near limit

Contribution remains negative

Society engagement is declining

Red

Critical unresolved incident

Repeated security-access failures

No backup sitter

Low resident demand

High refund/dispute rate

Society requests unsafe or unlawful practices

Significant privacy misuse

Red should trigger a formal review rather than automatic expansion.

6. Manual MVP dashboard

For the first 5–10 societies, a spreadsheet is acceptable.

Recommended tabs

1. Society Accounts

2. Resident Funnel

3. Bookings

4. Sitter Capacity

5. Complaints and Incidents

6. Finance

7. Events

8. Monthly Scorecard

9. Reference Lists

Society row example

### Table 135

| Society | Leads | Paid customers | Completed bookings | Repeat customers | Rating | Complaints | Monthly GMV | Health |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Green Acres | 42 | 18 | 28 | 7 | 4.7 | 1 | ₹36,800 | Green |

Move to an automated dashboard when

More than 10–15 societies are active

Manual updates become inconsistent

Multiple operations users edit the sheet

Real-time alerts are needed

Finance reconciliation becomes complex

Society users need controlled login access

Part B — Society Partnership Agreement

7. Agreement structure

A proper partnership agreement should clearly separate:

PetSaathi’s society-management obligations

Resident-to-PetSaathi service transactions

Sitter/provider responsibilities

Professional partner responsibilities

Society access responsibilities

Under Section 10 of the Indian Contract Act, an agreement becomes a contract where the parties have free consent, are competent to contract, and the agreement has lawful consideration and a lawful object. The final document should be reviewed by an Indian lawyer authorised to advise on the relevant state, entity type and society structure.

8. Parties and authority

The document should identify:

PetSaathi’s legal entity

Registered/business address

Authorised PetSaathi signatory

Society/RWA/AOA legal or operating name

Society address

Authorised society signatory

Signatory’s authority

Effective date

Do not sign only with an informal resident volunteer unless that person has authority to bind the society.

9. Service scope

Specify exactly which services are included.

Example:

Daily dog walking

In-home pet sitting

Controlled boarding beta

Resident booking assistance

Society sitter-access coordination

Report Card delivery

Complaint and incident support

Partner-event coordination

Also specify unavailable or excluded services:

Veterinary diagnosis or treatment by PetSaathi

Animal-control enforcement

Removal of community animals

Guaranteed sitter availability

Unlimited 24/7 support

Unlimited events

Automatic boarding approval

10. Resident booking process

Define the complete resident flow:

Resident registers voluntarily

↓

Residency is verified

↓

Pet Profile is completed

↓

Service request is submitted

↓

PetSaathi reviews eligibility

↓

Sitter is assigned

↓

Resident pays

↓

Payment is verified

↓

Booking is confirmed

The agreement should clarify:

The society is not purchasing each resident service unless expressly agreed.

Residents pay PetSaathi directly for their bookings.

Individual resident terms and policies apply separately.

The society does not guarantee service availability.

PetSaathi controls final caregiver assignment.

11. Sitter access process

Define:

Information provided to security

Advance-registration period

Approved entrance

Permitted service windows

Photo and PetSaathi ID

Access validity

Replacement-sitter procedure

Entry denial escalation

Access revocation

Security should see only operationally necessary fields:

Display name

Photograph

PetSaathi sitter ID

Approved service

Access validity

PetSaathi support contact

The society should not receive raw KYC files, bank information or unrelated sitter records.

12. Security-gate coordination

The agreement should assign responsibilities clearly.

PetSaathi

Provide accurate active-roster information

Notify replacements

Revoke removed sitter access

Maintain operations support

Inform the society of material booking-access changes

Society

Communicate gate requirements

Train relevant security staff

Allow properly approved sitters to enter

Notify PetSaathi of rule changes

Provide a route for urgent access escalation

Important protection

PetSaathi should not guarantee entry where:

The resident did not provide required approval

The society changed rules without notice

Security systems are unavailable

A government or emergency restriction applies

13. Payment model

State separately:

Society payments

Setup fee

Monthly subscription

Event coordination fees

Taxes

Payment due date

Late-payment consequences

Resident payments

Service pricing

Payment provider

Prepayment requirement

Discounts

Cancellation fees

Refund process

Partner payments

Groomer/veterinarian/trainer payout

Calculation basis

Tax and invoicing responsibility

Settlement period

Refund adjustments

Do not use undefined language such as:

“Commission will be charged.”

Specify the payer, recipient, amount or percentage, and calculation basis.

14. Refund and cancellation policy

Define separately:

Resident cancellation

Sitter cancellation

PetSaathi cancellation

Society access failure

Event cancellation

Weather or force-majeure situations

Partial service

Full or partial refund

Credit option

Replacement option

The Consumer Protection (E-Commerce) Rules apply to goods and services sold through electronic systems and include consumer-information and grievance obligations. PetSaathi should clearly disclose pricing, payment, refund, cancellation and grievance arrangements.

Consumers can also escalate grievances through the Department of Consumer Affairs’ National Consumer Helpline, which currently operates through its portal, telephone number 1915 and multiple digital channels.

15. Complaint escalation

The agreement should define three levels.

Level 1 — Routine issue

Examples:

Booking question

Minor delay

Report Card correction

Billing clarification

Target:

Same-day acknowledgement

Level 2 — Material service complaint

Examples:

Repeated lateness

Sitter communication failure

Access problem

Customer alleges poor handling

Target:

Immediate case owner

Formal investigation

Written resolution

Level 3 — Critical safety incident

Examples:

Injury

Bite

Escape

Medical emergency

Serious misconduct

Target:

Immediate escalation

Owner contacted

Emergency/veterinary coordination

Incident preservation

Senior safety review

The agreement should distinguish response time from final resolution time.

16. Emergency protocol

Define:

Who may report an emergency

PetSaathi emergency contact

Society/security contact

Owner and emergency-contact process

Veterinary coordination

Transport coordination

Spending authorisation

Evidence and incident records

Post-incident review

Important wording:

PetSaathi coordinates with owners, society contacts and registered veterinary partners. PetSaathi does not provide veterinary diagnosis or treatment unless such services are separately delivered by qualified professionals.

17. Data privacy and confidentiality

The agreement should define:

Data PetSaathi collects

Resident contact details

Society membership information

Pet Profiles

Booking information

Service media

Payment status

Complaints and incidents

Data the society receives

Aggregate bookings

Programme usage

On-time rate

Entry failures

Aggregate complaints

Approved sitter-access roster

Data the society does not automatically receive

Pet medical information

Emergency contacts

Individual payment details

Full GPS routes

Private media

Internal risk assessments

Individual complaint narratives

Include:

Permitted purposes

Access restrictions

Retention

deletion/return after agreement termination

Security expectations

Data-breach communication

Resident-request handling

Subprocessors or service providers where applicable

The DPDP framework requires reasonable security safeguards and provides for notification relating to personal-data breaches. The 2025 Rules also contemplate clear notices and published grievance/contact mechanisms.

18. No pet-ban and no enforcement positioning

This should be a dedicated contract clause.

Recommended clause

PetSaathi is engaged solely as a pet-care service and operational coordination provider. Nothing in this Agreement authorises PetSaathi to formulate, enforce or assist in enforcing blanket prohibitions on lawful pet ownership, compel residents to surrender or remove owned pets, remove community animals, impose breed-based prohibitions, or act as an animal-control, municipal, policing or adjudicatory authority. Any society rules or actions concerning animals remain subject to applicable law, official animal-welfare guidance and competent governmental or judicial authority.

Add operational clarification

PetSaathi may reasonably:

Refuse an individual service that cannot be delivered safely

Require complete Pet Profile information

Require suitable equipment

Pause matching after an incident

Restrict a sitter’s access

Apply service-specific safety controls

This is different from banning a resident from owning a pet.

AWBI’s official guidance includes a dedicated RWA/AOA circular and states that blanket bans on keeping pet dogs are not legally permissible.

19. Pilot duration and review

Define:

Pilot start date

Pilot end date

Booking cap

Active services

Pilot success metrics

Review meeting date

No automatic renewal

Recommended pilot duration:

21–30 days

Success measures should include:

Leads

Paid bookings

Completed services

Rating

Repeat customers

Security-entry success

Complaints

Sitter capacity

Society feedback

20. Renewal and conversion

At the end of the pilot, the society should move to one of these states:

CONVERT_TO_BASIC

CONVERT_TO_PREMIUM

CONTINUE_PILOT

EVENT_ONLY

PAUSE_AND_FIX

END_PARTNERSHIP

The agreement should state:

Renewal fee

New term

Renewal notice

Price-review process

Package changes

Additional-service pricing

Termination notice

Do not automatically charge the society after a free pilot unless it has expressly accepted the paid plan.

21. Branding and communication permission

Define whether PetSaathi may use:

Society name

Society logo

Photographs of premises

Event photographs

Committee testimonials

Resident testimonials

Partnership announcement

Require separate permission for resident names, pet photographs and testimonials.

The society should not use PetSaathi branding in a way that implies:

Government approval

Veterinary certification

Guaranteed safety

PetSaathi enforcement authority

Exclusivity unless contracted

22. Termination and immediate suspension

Ordinary termination

Example:

30 days’ written notice

Immediate pause or termination

Possible reasons:

Critical safety obstruction

Unauthorised resident-data disclosure

Repeated denial of approved sitter access

Non-payment

Fraud

Misuse of brand

Illegal or discriminatory demands

Interference with incident investigation

Repeated operational non-cooperation

On termination:

Stop accepting new society-specific bookings

Complete or cancel existing bookings appropriately

Revoke access rosters

Remove public partnership claims

Reconcile payments

Handle retained data according to policy and law

23. Agreement schedules

Keep the main contract readable by moving operational detail into schedules.

Schedule A — Active Services

Schedule B — Pricing and Payment

Schedule C — Sitter Access SOP

Schedule D — Data Sharing and Privacy

Schedule E — Support and Escalation

Schedule F — Pilot Metrics

Schedule G — Branding Permissions

This allows PetSaathi to update prices or service windows without rewriting the entire legal document, subject to the agreed amendment process.

24. End-to-end society operating cycle

Agreement signed

↓

Society configured

↓

Resident announcement approved

↓

Residents register voluntarily

↓

Services begin

↓

Dashboard updates daily

↓

Issues and incidents tracked

↓

Monthly society report generated

↓

Performance review held

↓

Renew, upgrade, fix or end

The dashboard provides evidence. The agreement provides authority and boundaries.

25. Definition of done

Dashboard

The dashboard is ready when:

Every metric has a precise definition.

Paid, confirmed and completed bookings are separate.

Repeat interest and actual repeat services are separate.

GMV, subscription fees and contribution are separate.

Complaints and incidents are separate.

Society management sees aggregate data only.

Every red alert has an owner and due date.

Monthly data can be audited back to source records.

Agreement

The agreement is ready when:

Signatory authority is confirmed.

Service scope and exclusions are clear.

Resident bookings are separate from society fees.

Sitter access responsibilities are defined.

Refund and complaint processes are documented.

Emergency responsibilities are realistic.

Privacy and data-sharing boundaries are explicit.

No-pet-ban/no-enforcement language is included.

Renewal and termination are clear.

An Indian lawyer has reviewed the final version.

Final operating principle

The dashboard should prove whether the partnership is creating demand, reliable services, repeat usage and positive value. The agreement should prevent operational confusion, privacy misuse and any attempt to turn PetSaathi into a society pet-control authority.

Simple explanation for professor

“The society dashboard will track the performance of every PetSaathi society partnership.

It will show resident leads, active customers, monthly bookings, repeat customers, customer ratings, complaints, sitter availability, revenue and event participation. Paid booking requests and completed services will be reported separately so that the dashboard does not exaggerate performance.

The society management will see only aggregate information. Individual pet medical details, private Report Cards, payment data and emergency contacts will remain restricted to authorised PetSaathi users.

The partnership agreement will explain which services PetSaathi provides, how residents book, how sitters enter the society, how payments and refunds work, and how complaints or emergencies are handled.

The agreement will also contain an important boundary: PetSaathi is a pet-care service provider. It is not an animal-control authority and will not create or enforce blanket pet bans.

At the end of every month, PetSaathi will use the dashboard to review demand, service quality, complaints, sitter supply and financial value. Based on this information, the society can continue, upgrade, pause or end the partnership.”

A monthly AWBI, DPDP and consumer-rule compliance watch would help keep the dashboard controls and agreement clauses current.

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Correct Society Partnership Strategy and Gate Protocol 🏢🐾

Executive decision

The corrected society model is not:

PetSaathi → RWA approval → launch

It is:

Pet-parent demand

+

RWA/facility approval

+

Security access workflow

+

Local sitter capacity

+

Veterinary/partner ecosystem

↓

Operational society partnership

PetSaathi should be positioned as a pet-care service provider and coordination platform, not an animal-control or society-rule enforcement agency. AWBI guidance states that RWAs and apartment-owner associations cannot introduce blanket bans on residents keeping pet dogs, and it promotes reasonable arrangements for responsible community living.

Mistake 1 — Pitching only to the RWA

Wrong approach

Convince RWA secretary

↓

Sign agreement

↓

Assume residents will book

An RWA can permit communication and help establish access procedures, but it does not create resident demand by itself.

Correct stakeholder model

1. Pet parents create demand

Pet parents determine whether there is a real need for:

Daily dog walking

Office-hour pet sitting

Travel care

Same-sitter plans

Festival support

Grooming or veterinary-partner camps

Before launching, obtain evidence such as:

10+ interested pet-parent households

or

5+ residents ready for a paid trial

2. RWA or committee provides programme approval

The committee should approve:

Resident communication

Pilot scope

Partnership duration

Society branding

Aggregate reporting

Commercial terms

It should not receive unrestricted access to Pet Profiles, medical data or individual payment details.

3. Facility manager makes the system operational

The facility manager helps define:

Visitor-entry procedure

Gates and towers

Approved service times

Lift or common-area procedures

Event permissions

Security escalation

Replacement-sitter access

4. Security executes access

Security confirms that the arriving person matches the booking-specific access record.

Security should not decide:

Whether the sitter is suitable for the pet

Pet risk classification

Refund eligibility

Customer pricing

Whether the booking should proceed medically

5. Local partners strengthen credibility

Veterinarians, groomers, pet shops and trainers may help with:

Warm society introductions

Professional events

Emergency coordination

Resident education

Partner referrals

Correct sequence

Validate pet-parent interest

↓

Meet RWA/facility manager

↓

Confirm operational feasibility

↓

Design security access

↓

Launch limited pilot

Mistake 2 — Asking for a paid contract too early

Wrong approach

“Pay ₹25,000 per month before your residents have used the service.”

This creates excessive decision risk for the society.

Correct conversion model

No-fee setup pilot

↓

Resident opt-in

↓

Paid individual bookings

↓

Report Cards and ratings

↓

Repeat bookings

↓

Pilot performance report

↓

Paid society partnership

Recommended pilot

### Table 136

| Item | Pilot rule |
| --- | --- |
| Duration | 21–30 days |
| Society setup fee | ₹0 |
| Society partnership fee | ₹0 |
| Resident services | Paid |
| Primary services | Dog walking and home sitting |
| Capacity | Limited by available sitters |
| Renewal | Not automatic |

The phrase “free pilot” must be explained carefully: the society pays no programme fee, while residents continue paying for the individual care services they book.

Paid-conversion evidence

Before proposing a paid package, show:

Opted-in residents

Completed Pet Profiles

Paid bookings

Completed services

On-time rate

Report Card delivery

Average rating

Repeat bookings

Entry failures

Complaints and resolutions

A society should pay for demonstrated operational value—not a theoretical feature list.

Mistake 3 — Using a generic website link

Wrong approach

petsaathi.in

The resident then has to discover:

Whether PetSaathi serves their society

Which services are available

Whether sitters can enter

Which offer applies

How support works

This creates unnecessary friction.

Correct approach

Create a society-specific page:

/pet-care/societies/ahmedabad/green-acres-bopal

The page should show

Society name and programme status

Active services

Resident offer

Local sitter summaries

Society access process

Supported service windows

Booking CTA

Support contact

Report Card example

Society-specific FAQs

Partner veterinarian or groomer

Current availability

Booking improvement

When the resident clicks Book Now, automatically attach:

society_id

society_service_config

resident_offer_id

access_rule_version

The resident should not have to search for the society or type the complete address again.

Privacy boundary

The public page may display:

Society name

General area

Public sitter summaries

Service windows

Resident offer

Support channel

It must not display:

Resident lists

Flat numbers

Access codes

Personal sitter phone numbers

Pet medical details

Full GPS routes

The final DPDP Rules require clear notices describing the personal data being processed and its purpose, while also specifying safeguards such as access controls, masking, logging and security measures. The rules were published in November 2025 with staged commencement provisions.

Mistake 4 — Treating pets as a society conflict

Wrong positioning

“We solve pet complaints for societies.”

This implies that pets or pet owners are the problem and risks turning PetSaathi into an enforcement mechanism.

Correct positioning

PetSaathi helps residents access reliable pet care while improving service-provider access, cleanliness coordination, service evidence and issue escalation within the community.

Balanced stakeholder message

For pet parents

Reliable care

Verified sitter information

Service proof

Repeat options

Emergency coordination

For non-pet residents

Clear service times

Responsible common-area procedures

Waste-cleanup expectations

Complaint route

Less unmanaged service-provider movement

For society management

Approved-access roster

Booking verification

Aggregate reporting

Formal incident process

Clear operations contact

For security

Booking-specific entry proof

Current sitter photograph

Access validity

Clear replacement procedure

AWBI guidance says blanket pet bans are not permissible, while also advising responsible practices such as leashing in common areas, cleanliness and mutually acceptable arrangements. PetSaathi should support that balanced operating model.

Recommended agreement clause

PetSaathi is engaged solely as a pet-care service provider and operational coordination platform. PetSaathi shall not act as an animal-control authority, pet-rule enforcement agency, municipal authority or adjudicator of disputes concerning lawful pet ownership.

The Society shall not use the PetSaathi programme to impose blanket pet bans, breed-based restrictions, unlawful removal requirements or discriminatory conditions against pet-owning residents.

PetSaathi may support reasonable service-access, cleanliness, safety, communication and incident-escalation procedures consistent with applicable law, animal-welfare guidance and the terms of this Agreement.

Mistake 5 — No sitter-access planning

Wrong approach

Booking confirmed

↓

Sitter reaches gate

↓

Security has no information

↓

Sitter is refused entry

↓

Customer, sitter and security begin calling everyone

This creates delays, cancellations and safety confusion.

Correct operating principle

A booking is not operationally ready until the society access record is also ready.

The access workflow must link:

Society

+ booking

+ resident

+ assigned sitter

+ scheduled service window

End-to-end gate and security protocol

Stage 1 — Society access configuration

Before the pilot launches, create a society_access_rules record containing:

Approved gate

Visitor-registration requirement

Sitter-ID format

Advance-notice period

Service hours

Tower or block process

Entry/exit logging

Replacement-sitter procedure

Emergency-entry process

Security escalation contact

Stage 2 — Maintain the active sitter roster

Security receives a restricted roster with:

Sitter display name

Current photograph

PetSaathi sitter ID

Approved service category

Access status

Valid-from date

Expiry date

PetSaathi operations contact

Security should not receive:

Full KYC document copies

Bank information

Home address

Internal incident history

Ratings investigation

Other customers’ bookings

Stage 3 — Booking-specific access record

When the booking is confirmed, create:

access_record_id

booking_id

society_id

resident_unit_reference

assigned_sitter_id

service_type

scheduled_start

scheduled_end

access_status

Recommended access statuses:

NOT_REQUIRED

PENDING

PRE_APPROVED

ARRIVED

ENTRY_APPROVED

ENTRY_DENIED

SERVICE_IN_PROGRESS

EXIT_RECORDED

CANCELLED

EXPIRED

Stage 4 — Pre-arrival notification

Send the security desk a booking-specific notification before the service.

PetSaathi Access Notice

Booking: [Booking ID]Society: [Society Name]Service: [Dog Walking / Pet Sitting]Date: [Date]Service window: [Start Time]–[End Time]

Assigned caregiver:Name: [Sitter Name]PetSaathi ID: [Sitter ID]Photo: [Secure Photo Link / App Record]Access status: Pre-approved

Resident:Tower/Block: [Tower]Flat: [Flat Number]

Please verify the sitter’s PetSaathi ID and photograph at entry.

For changes, replacement confirmation or support, contact:[PetSaathi Operations Number]

This notice is valid only for the stated booking and service window.

Stage 5 — Sitter arrival

The sitter opens the booking in the PetSaathi app and shows:

Current photograph

PetSaathi ID

Booking ID

Society name

Resident tower and flat

Service type

Valid service window

Dynamic verification code or QR, where available

The gate should not depend only on a text message that could be forwarded or edited.

Corrected sitter gate message

Your original message is directionally correct, but the sitter’s personal phone number should not be the main verification method.

Hello, I’m [Sitter Name], PetSaathi ID [Sitter ID].

I am here for confirmed booking [Booking ID].

Society: [Society Name]Resident: [Resident First Name]Tower/Block: [Tower]Flat: [Flat Number]Service: [Dog Walking / Pet Sitting]Approved service window: [Start Time]–[End Time]

Please verify my photograph and booking in the PetSaathi access record.

For confirmation or any mismatch, contact PetSaathi Operations:[Operations Number]

Stage 6 — Gate verification

Security checks:

Photo matches sitter

PetSaathi ID matches

Booking ID exists

Date and time are valid

Society and unit match

Access status is PRE_APPROVED

Booking has not been cancelled

If everything matches:

access_status = ENTRY_APPROVED

actual_entry_at = timestamp

Stage 7 — Resident confirmation

For first-time services or high-risk access:

Send the resident an arrival notification.

Let the resident approve through the app or existing visitor-management process.

Record the approval timestamp.

Do not require the resident to share OTPs in public groups.

Stage 8 — Service start

Gate entry does not automatically start the pet-care service.

The sitter must separately confirm:

Pet handover completed

Correct pet identified

Equipment checked

Instructions reviewed

Service started

This preserves the distinction between:

Building entry

and

Pet-care service start

Stage 9 — Exit

At completion:

Sitter checks out of the booking.

Security records departure where required.

PetSaathi closes the temporary access record.

The resident receives the Report Card.

Access expires automatically.

Recommended rule:

access validity =

scheduled window

+ approved arrival buffer

+ approved completion buffer

Do not leave indefinite access active after the booking.

Replacement-sitter protocol

A replacement should never arrive using the original sitter’s access approval.

Correct flow

Primary sitter removed

↓

Original access record revoked

↓

Replacement verified

↓

Customer notified

↓

New booking assignment recorded

↓

New access record issued

↓

Security notified

Security message

The original caregiver is no longer authorised for booking [Booking ID]. Do not permit entry using the previous notice. A new access notice will be issued for the approved replacement.

Cancellation protocol

When a booking is cancelled:

booking_status = CANCELLED

access_status = CANCELLED

security notified

sitter notified

resident notified

A cancelled booking must not remain valid at the gate.

Emergency-entry protocol

During a genuine active-service emergency, the operations team may need to:

Contact security

Request expedited exit or entry

Coordinate pet transport

Contact the owner

Contact a veterinary partner

Preserve an incident timeline

Emergency access must still identify:

Incident ID

Booking ID

Authorised responder

Purpose

Operations contact

Do not use “emergency” to bypass ordinary verification for routine delays.

Gate data-visibility matrix

### Table 137

| Information | Security | Assigned sitter | Society committee |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Sitter name/photo | Yes | Self | Limited roster |
| PetSaathi sitter ID | Yes | Yes | Limited roster |
| Booking ID | Yes | Yes | Aggregate only |
| Resident tower/flat | Yes, booking-specific | Yes, after assignment | No default access |
| Resident phone | Prefer masked/operations relay | Masked or required contact | No |
| Service time | Yes | Yes | Aggregate |
| Payment amount | No | No need | No |
| Pet medical details | No | Relevant care only | No |
| Pet handling risks | No, unless entry safety is affected | Relevant controls | No |
| GPS route | No | During assignment | No |
| Emergency contact | PetSaathi operations | Relevant authorised contacts | No default access |
| Complaint details | No | Only when relevant | Aggregate only |

The safest design is purpose-based access: each role receives only the information required for its specific function. DPDP rules require clear processing purposes and contemplate security controls such as masking, restricted access and logging.

Store it internally, but do not rely on the sitter’s phone number as gate proof.

Deny temporary entry

↓

↓

Wait according to policy.

Contact operations.

Record denial reason.

Notify customer.

Preserve evidence for society review.

Society-partnership success model

The corrected funnel is:

Pet-parent demand confirmed

↓

RWA/facility approval

↓

Security protocol configured

↓

Society-specific page launched

↓

Residents opt in

↓

Key metrics

Stakeholder health

### Table 138

| Metric | Target |
| --- | --- |
| Residents opting in | Increasing |

Definition of done

The society partnership is operationally ready only when:

Resident demand has been validated.

An authorised society contact has approved the pilot.

The facility manager has reviewed the workflow.

Security has a current sitter roster.

Booking-specific access notices work.

Replacement access revokes the original approval.

Cancelled bookings automatically lose access.

Private resident data is restricted.

The society-specific landing page is active.

Residents can register voluntarily.

Every service produces a Report Card.

PetSaathi is not used as a pet-rule enforcement agency.

Simple explanation for professor

“Many society partnership strategies fail because founders speak only to the RWA manager.

A successful society partnership needs several stakeholders. Pet parents create real service demand. The RWA gives programme approval. The facility manager explains operational requirements. Security controls sitter entry, and local veterinarians or pet shops improve trust and referrals.

PetSaathi should begin with a 21-to-30-day pilot instead of immediately asking for a large paid contract. The pilot should produce real registrations, paid bookings, ratings and repeat services. After demonstrating results, PetSaathi can offer the society a monthly paid package.

Each society should have a personalised booking page rather than a generic website link. It will show the available services, local sitters, resident offer, booking process and support contact.

PetSaathi should not describe pets as a society problem. It should explain that it helps pet parents access reliable care while improving service-provider entry, cleanliness coordination and complaint escalation.

The most important operational feature is the gate protocol. Before a service, security receives the sitter’s name, photograph, PetSaathi ID, booking ID, resident tower and flat, service type and valid time window. Security checks this information before entry.

If the sitter changes, the original access permission is cancelled and a new approval is issued. If the booking is cancelled, the gate approval is also cancelled.

Security receives only the information needed for access. It does not receive the pet’s medical history, customer payment details or private emergency contacts.

This makes the society partnership safe, organised and scalable while keeping PetSaathi clearly positioned as a pet-care provider—not an animal-control authority.”

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Society Marketing Assets and Technical Product Architecture 🏢🐾

Executive decision

Your proposed marketing and technical structure is directionally correct, but it needs four production-level improvements:

Marketing claims must be precise. Avoid presenting “verified,” “safe,” “emergency support,” or “from ₹99” without explaining the actual checks, limitations and eligibility.

Society membership must be separate from customer identity. One customer may move societies, belong to multiple communities or have verification revoked.

Pricing, reports and contacts need versioned relational tables. They should not be embedded as loosely maintained columns inside the main societies table.

Public, resident and admin APIs must return different data shapes. A public society endpoint must never expose resident, contact, pricing-rule or internal operational fields.

ASCI’s advertising code requires promotional representations to be truthful and not misleading. Therefore, every poster, brochure and landing-page claim should match the actual verification, availability and service workflow.

Part A — Society marketing assets

1. Marketing objective

The marketing system should move a resident through this funnel:

Poster or society announcement

↓

Resident scans society QR code

↓

Society landing page opens

↓

Resident verifies membership

↓

Resident creates/selects Pet Profile

↓

Resident views society offer

↓

Booking request submitted

↓

Payment verified

↓

Service completed

↓

Report Card delivered

↓

Repeat plan offered

Every marketing asset should therefore have:

One clear society identity

One main service promise

One trust explanation

One offer

One QR-based action

One support contact

Visible conditions

2. Poster headline strategy

Your original headline ideas are good, but “safe” and “verified” should be used carefully.

Recommended dog-walking headline

Screened dog walkers now available for your society 🐾

Supporting line:

Book 30- or 60-minute walks with approved-access caregivers, service updates and a Pet Report Card.

CTA:

Scan to view available walking slots

This is stronger than implying that every walker is permanently available or that the service is risk-free.

Recommended pet-sitting headline

Going out? Arrange trusted in-home pet sitting.

Supporting line:

Receive food, water, routine and photo updates from an eligible PetSaathi sitter.

CTA:

Scan to book society pet care

Avoid:

“Leave your pet completely worry-free.”

That is an absolute emotional promise that cannot realistically be guaranteed.

Recommended busy-morning headline

Busy morning? Keep your dog’s routine on track.

Supporting line:

Request a local dog walker with society-access registration and a service Report Card.

CTA:

Check morning availability

Recommended trial-offer headline

Your first eligible 30-minute dog walk from ₹99

Conditions should appear immediately beneath it:

One introductory offer per verified household. Subject to Pet Profile review, sitter availability, selected time slot and pilot capacity.

Use “from ₹99” only when a genuine ₹99 booking option is actually available. Promotional claims and price representations should be accurate and supportable.

3. QR poster structure

A good society poster should contain only the information necessary to create action.

Recommended layout

Top section

PetSaathi × Green Acres Bopal

Main headline

Screened dog walking and pet sitting

for Green Acres residents

Trust points

✓ Society-access caregiver roster

✓ Booking and service updates

✓ Photo updates where applicable

✓ Pet Report Card after service

✓ Structured support and escalation

Offer

First eligible dog walk from ₹99

CTA

Scan to verify residency and book

[QR CODE]

Footer

Services are subject to Pet Profile review,

caregiver eligibility, payment and availability.

Emergency support means escalation and

veterinary coordination through available partners.

&utm_medium=qr

5. Brochure structure

The brochure should be short—ideally two or four pages.

Page 1 — Society-specific introduction

Headline:

Reliable pet care, right inside your community

Include:

Society name

Active services

30- or 60-minute options

Page 3 — How verification works

Explain verification in layers:

Identity reviewed

↓

Interview completed

↓

↓

Training reviewed

↓

Society access registered

↓

Performance monitored

Do not suggest that one identity document guarantees service safety.

PetSaathi uses multiple screening and operational controls. No service can be guaranteed to be risk-free.

Page 4 — Booking, pricing and support

Include:

PetSaathi reviews care requirements

Also display:

6. Report Card example

Use a fictional or expressly consented example.

PET REPORT CARD 🐾

Pet: Bruno

Service: 30-minute dog walk

Sitter: Riya

Time: 7:30 AM–8:02 AM

Approximate distance: 1.4 km

Water: Given

Pee/poop: Yes

Mood: Happy and energetic

Leash behaviour: Calm after first five minutes

Concern: None reported

Sitter note:

Bruno enjoyed the walk and returned home comfortably.

Do not expose a real resident’s pet or booking information in a public brochure without permission.

7. Emergency-support wording

Replace:

Emergency support

with:

Priority service escalation and veterinary coordination

PetSaathi may:

Contact the pet owner

Contact saved emergency contacts

Contact an available registered veterinary partner

Coordinate transport where available

Create an incident record

Inform society security where operationally necessary

PetSaathi should not imply that ordinary support staff diagnose, prescribe medication or provide veterinary treatment.

Part B — Correct technical architecture

9. Your original schema: what needs correction

The original tables are useful as a concept, but they combine unrelated responsibilities.

Original problem

societies

- contact_person

- contact_phone

A society can have:

Multiple RWA contacts

Facility manager

Security supervisor

Emergency contact

Finance contact

Historical contacts

Therefore, contacts should be separate records.

Original problem

society_residents

- flat_number

- verification_status

This lacks:

Membership type

Verification method

Expiry

Approval history

Revocation

Multiple occupants

Tenant/owner distinction

Original problem

society_pricing

- resident_price

- normal_price

This lacks:

Currency

Effective dates

Duration

Extra-pet fee

Peak pricing

Tax treatment

Offer limits

Version history

Original problem

society_reports

- revenue

- complaints

This is ambiguous:

Is revenue GMV or PetSaathi revenue?

Is complaints a count or text?

Were cancellations included?

What reporting period was used?

Can the calculation be reproduced?

10. Recommended core entities

societies

society_contacts

society_memberships

society_access_rules

society_service_configs

society_pricing_rules

society_offers

society_sitter_access

society_partners

society_events

society_report_snapshots

society_status_history

Use database foreign keys and unique constraints to protect relationships and prevent duplicates. PostgreSQL foreign keys maintain referential integrity between related records, while primary-key and unique constraints enforce identity and uniqueness at the database level.

20. Next.js page architecture

Your original pages:

/societies

/societies/[city]

/societies/[slug]

should be changed to:

/societies

/societies/[city]

/societies/[city]/[societySlug]

This avoids slug collisions such as two societies named “Green Acres” in different cities.

Next.js App Router supports dynamic segments such as [city] and [societySlug], and passes those values to pages, layouts, Route Handlers and generateMetadata.

Recommended files

app/

├── societies/

│ ├── page.tsx

│ ├── [city]/

│ │ ├── page.tsx

│ │ └── [societySlug]/

│ │ ├── page.tsx

│ │ ├── loading.tsx

│ │ ├── not-found.tsx

│ │ └── opengraph-image.tsx

│

├── customer/

│ └── societies/

│ ├── join/

│ └── [membershipId]/

│

└── admin/

└── societies/

├── page.tsx

└── [id]/

├── page.tsx

├── residents/

├── sitters/

├── pricing/

├── reports/

├── events/

└── settings/

Use generateMetadata() for society-specific titles, descriptions, canonical URLs and social-sharing metadata.

21. Society-page SEO protection

Do not automatically index every society record.

Index only when:

partnership_status is active

AND public_page_status = PUBLIC

AND services are active

AND page contains meaningful local information

Keep draft and low-information pilot pages as:

noindex, follow

Google considers pages created at scale with substantially similar content and little unique value to be potential doorway or scaled-content abuse. Society pages should contain actual local services, operating windows, offers, FAQs, partners and access information.

22. Correct API architecture

Next.js App Router Route Handlers support custom GET, POST, PATCH, DELETE and other HTTP handlers inside the app directory.

Public APIs

GET /api/public/societies

GET /api/public/societies/[city]/[slug]

GET /api/public/societies/[id]/services

GET /api/public/societies/[id]/sitters

GET /api/public/societies/[id]/offers

GET /api/public/societies/[id]/events

GET /api/public/societies/[id]/faqs

These should return only approved public fields.

Resident membership APIs

POST /api/societies/[id]/memberships/start

POST /api/societies/[id]/memberships/verify-phone

POST /api/societies/[id]/memberships/redeem-invite

POST /api/societies/[id]/memberships/submit

GET /api/me/society-memberships

GET /api/me/society-memberships/[id]

DELETE /api/me/society-memberships/[id]

Avoid the vague endpoint:

POST /api/societies/:id/join

because joining involves several states and verification actions.

Admin society APIs

POST /api/admin/societies

GET /api/admin/societies

GET /api/admin/societies/[id]

PATCH /api/admin/societies/[id]

POST /api/admin/societies/[id]/activate-pilot

POST /api/admin/societies/[id]/pause

POST /api/admin/societies/[id]/end

Use PATCH for partial updates rather than replacing the entire society resource with PUT.

Admin membership APIs

GET /api/admin/societies/[id]/memberships

GET /api/admin/society-memberships/[membershipId]

POST /api/admin/society-memberships/[membershipId]/approve

POST /api/admin/society-memberships/[membershipId]/reject

POST /api/admin/society-memberships/[membershipId]/revoke

Pricing and offer APIs

GET /api/admin/societies/[id]/pricing

Report APIs

POST /api/admin/societies/[id]/reports/generate

GET /api/admin/societies/[id]/reports

GET /api/admin/society-reports/[reportId]

The live metrics endpoint calculates current data.

The report endpoint creates a permanent monthly snapshot.

23. API security rules

Never trust a user-supplied society ID, membership ID or report ID without checking authorization.

OWASP identifies broken object-level authorization as a major API risk: attackers may change an object identifier and gain access to another user’s resource unless every endpoint performs an object-level permission check.

Required checks

Public user

May access:

Public society page

Public services

Public sitter summaries

Public event details

Verified resident

May access:

Their society offers

Resident-only service slots

Their own membership

Their own bookings

Society representative

May access:

Aggregate programme information

Access roster

Approved society events

Membership approval requests only where contractually authorised

May not access:

Pet medical data

Individual payment records

Private media

Full complaints

Detailed risk assessments

Admin

Must still have role-specific permissions:

SOCIETY_OPERATIONS

SAFETY_ADMIN

FINANCE_ADMIN

PARTNER_MANAGER

SUPER_ADMIN

Also return different DTOs:

PublicSocietyResponse

ResidentSocietyResponse

SocietyManagerResponse

AdminSocietyResponse

OWASP also warns against exposing sensitive object properties merely because the user may access the underlying record.

Use transactions for state changes that update multiple related records. Prisma documents transaction handling and warns that concurrent writes can create conflicts or unique-constraint failures if they are not coordinated correctly.

29. Definition of done

Marketing assets

Poster claim matches actual service.

Trial terms are visible.

Society name/logo usage is authorised.

QR points to the correct society page.

Emergency wording is accurate.

Resident privacy is not exposed.

Asset source and campaign are trackable.

Database

Society contacts are separate.

Membership verification is versioned.

Pricing has effective dates.

Monetary values use integer units.

Reports distinguish GMV, revenue and payouts.

Foreign keys and unique constraints exist.

Status history is auditable.

API

Public APIs return public fields only.

Every ID-based endpoint checks authorization.

Membership workflow has separate endpoints.

Admin roles are separated.

Sensitive properties are filtered.

Invalid state transitions are rejected.

Pages

URL includes city and society.

Society services are genuinely active.

Resident offers require verification.

Unsupported services show waitlist or unavailable.

Thin draft pages are not indexed.

Mobile conversion works.

Customer journey

Resident verification works.

Pet Profile is reusable.

Society price is server-calculated.

Sitter matching uses society-access status.

Payment is required before confirmation.

Report Card and review work.

Repeat booking is simple.

Final operating principle

Society marketing should make one accurate promise, while the technical architecture ensures that only verified residents receive society benefits, only eligible sitters receive assignments and only authorised users see private information.

Simple explanation for professor

“PetSaathi will create society-specific posters, brochures and QR codes to help residents discover local dog walking and pet-sitting services.

Each QR code will open a personalised society page rather than the generic homepage. Residents will see the active services, society offer, sitter information, booking process and support contact.

The technical system will contain a main society record, but contacts, residents, pricing, sitters and reports will be stored in separate tables. This prevents one large table from becoming difficult to maintain.

Residents will not become verified simply by typing a flat number. They will verify their phone and then use a society invitation, code or admin approval. Only verified residents will see private offers and resident-only service slots.

The pricing system will store prices in paise and include effective dates, discounts and service duration. Monthly reports will separately show booking value, PetSaathi revenue, sitter payouts, refunds, ratings and complaints.

The public society page will use a city and society URL such as /societies/ahmedabad/green-acres-bopal. Draft or incomplete pages will not be indexed.

Every API will check whether the user is authorised to access the requested society, membership or report. Public APIs will return only public information, while resident and admin APIs will return different protected data.

The complete customer journey will be society selection, resident verification, Pet Profile, service selection, payment, sitter assignment, gate access, service completion, Report Card and repeat booking.”

PetSaathi Phase 7 — Final Deliverables and Upgrade Decisions 🏢🐾

Executive decision

Phase 7 should finish with a repeatable society partnership operating system, not merely a pitch deck and several society contacts.

The completed system should support this full journey:

Society identified

↓

Decision-maker contacted

↓

Resident demand validated

↓

Pilot agreement signed

↓

Society page and gate protocol configured

↓

Residents register voluntarily

↓

Paid bookings completed

↓

Report Cards and reviews collected

↓

Pilot results reported

↓

Society converts to a paid package

Recommended final target

5–10 signed pilot societies

3–5 fully active societies at the same time

Remaining signed societies launched in controlled batches

At least one society converted to a paid partnership

No unresolved critical safety, privacy or gate-access issue

Launching all ten simultaneously could overload sitter capacity, support and society-access operations.

1. Society pitch deck

Purpose

Give RWA members, facility managers and committee members a clear five-to-ten-minute explanation of:

The society-level problem

PetSaathi’s service model

Stakeholder benefits

Safety controls

Pilot offer

Packages and pricing

Launch process

Evidence and next step

Required slides

Problem

PetSaathi solution

Benefits for pet parents, management, security and residents

Verification and safety system

Active services

No-fee pilot

Paid packages

Launch process

Actual results or pilot success measures

Call to action

Acceptance criteria

No unsupported claims such as “100% safe” or “guaranteed sitter.”

Veterinary services are described as partner-led coordination.

“Free pilot” clearly means no society partnership fee; individual bookings remain paid.

Pricing matches the website, proposal and agreement.

Case-study data is real and traceable.

ASCI’s code requires advertising claims to be truthful and not misleading through exaggeration, implication or omission.

2. Society package pricing

The commercial ladder should be finalised before further outreach.

### Table 141

| Package | Recommended price | Best for |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No-Fee Society Pilot | ₹0 society fee for 21–30 days | New qualified societies |
| Basic Society Partner | ₹5,000–₹15,000/month + tax | Societies with repeat demand |
| Premium Pet-Care Desk | ₹15,000–₹50,000/month + tax | Large premium communities |
| Event/Camp Programme | Event-specific | Lead acquisition and activation |

Residents should continue to pay separately for the services they book.

Discount funding

Taxes

Refund responsibility

Included volume

Extra-work charges

Renewal and termination terms

Where PetSaathi is required to issue a taxable service invoice, CBIC rules prescribe invoice particulars and generally require service invoices within 30 days of supply. Final GST treatment should be confirmed with a chartered accountant.

Recommended route

/pet-care/societies/[city]/[society-slug]

SEO requirement

Society pages should become mandatory for active partners, but only when each page contains genuine local information. Publishing hundreds of pages where only the society name changes could be treated as doorway or scaled-content abuse by Google.

Use:

DRAFT/PILOT incomplete page → noindex

ACTIVE complete page → indexable

ENDED page → redirect or archive appropriately

Do not mark a society address as a PetSaathi LocalBusiness location unless PetSaathi genuinely operates a business branch there; Google’s local-business markup is intended to describe actual business locations and operating details.

5. Society outreach scripts

Required script library

RWA/committee WhatsApp message

Facility-manager message

Formal email

LinkedIn introduction

Resident champion request

Pet-parent group announcement

Veterinary/pet-shop referral message

Three follow-ups

Opt-out reply

Discovery-call agenda

Messaging rules

Every script should clarify:

What “verified” means

Which services are active

Whether boarding is beta

That veterinary services are partner-led

That residents pay for booked services

That availability is not guaranteed

What the recipient should do next

WhatsApp business messages require prior user opt-in. The opt-in should identify PetSaathi and the reason messages will be sent.

Acceptance criteria

No bulk cold WhatsApp outreach.

No imported resident phone lists.

Every message is society-specific.

Declines and opt-outs are respected.

The CTA requests one action, usually a 15-minute discussion.

6. Society pilot agreement

Purpose

Define the legal and operational relationship before residents are onboarded.

Required clauses

Parties and authorised representatives

Pilot start and end dates

Active services

Society fee and resident payments

Sitter assignment authority

Resident registration process

Security and gate coordination

Complaint escalation

Incident and emergency protocol

Data protection and reporting

Branding permission

Partner-led veterinary/grooming services

Refund and cancellation responsibilities

Suspension and termination

No automatic renewal

No guarantee of zero risk

Critical positioning clause

PetSaathi is a pet-care service and coordination platform. It is not an animal-control authority and will not enforce blanket pet bans, remove community animals or determine society pet-ownership rules.

AWBI’s published guidance states that RWAs and apartment-owner associations should not impose blanket bans on residents keeping pet dogs. PetSaathi’s agreement should support responsible coexistence rather than pet-control demands.

The final agreement should be reviewed by an Indian lawyer familiar with the society’s state, legal structure and local rules.

Replacement rule

A replacement sitter must not arrive with only a WhatsApp explanation.

The system should:

Approve the replacement.

Avoid displaying full emergency contacts or extensive resident information in a gate message.

Decision

Include it in Phase 7, but treat it as a controlled acquisition channel, not a requirement for every society.

The Indian Veterinary Council Act regulates veterinary practice and the registered-practitioner framework. Medical and vaccination camps should therefore be controlled by appropriately registered veterinary practitioners.

Acceptance criteria

Upgrade decisions

Upgrade 1 — Society-specific landing pages

Decision: Approve with conditions

Make a custom page mandatory for:

Active pilots

Paid partner societies

Event-only societies where public registration is needed

Do not require a public indexable page for every prospect.

Conditions

Real local services

Real availability

Real resident offer

Real FAQs and access rules

Unique society information

Private data excluded

Draft pages use noindex

Reason

A proper page improves trust and conversion. It may also support local discovery, but SEO should be treated as a secondary benefit. Google warns against large collections of substantially similar location pages created mainly for search rankings.

Upgrade 2 — 30-day free pilot before a paid plan

Decision: Approve as the default, not an absolute rule

Recommended default:

21–30 day no-fee society pilot

Use a pilot when

Society has not worked with PetSaathi before.

Resident demand is uncertain.

Gate/access needs testing.

Sitter density is unproven.

Committee needs evidence.

Allow exceptions when

A large property-management company requests a paid rollout.

A society already has strong verified resident demand.

The society only purchases an event/camp.

A developer or employer funds the programme.

The work requires substantial custom setup.

Conversion rule

Do not extend the pilot indefinitely.

At the end, choose:

CONVERT_TO_PAID

EXTEND_ONCE_WITH_REASON

PAUSE_AND_FIX

END_PILOT

Upgrade 3 — Society gate protocol

Decision: Approve and make mandatory

No society should be marked operationally active without:

Access requirements

Approved sitter roster

Booking-proof format

Replacement process

Revocation process

Security contact

Emergency escalation

Entry-data retention rule

Reason

A booking has operationally failed when the confirmed sitter cannot enter the property, even when the customer, payment and sitter are otherwise ready.

Upgrade 4 — Society pet-care report

Decision: Approve with stage-based frequency

### Table 143

| Partnership stage | Reporting requirement |
| --- | --- |
| No-fee pilot | End-of-pilot report |
| Pilot longer than 30 days | Monthly interim report |
| Basic Partner | Monthly report |
| Premium Desk | Monthly report plus review meeting |
| Event-only | Event performance report |

Reason

Reporting provides evidence for:

Paid conversion

Renewal

Pricing

Capacity planning

Complaint management

Resident value

Society return on the partnership

Reports must remain aggregated and privacy-controlled.

Upgrade 5 — Event/camp acquisition

Decision: Approve as a selective Phase 7 channel

Do not make events mandatory for every society.

Use them when:

Offline trust matters.

Resident awareness is low.

A strong local partner exists.

The society wants community activation.

Recurring service demand needs stimulation.

Do not use them when:

Core booking operations are unstable.

No qualified partner exists.

Events would overload support staff.

The society expects medical services from PetSaathi staff.

Event economics are negative with no credible follow-up conversion.

Priority order

Dog-walking trial day

Pet-parent orientation

Grooming day

Veterinary-partner health session

Vaccination camp with registered veterinary control

Training/puppy workshop
